## The layout of the repository and the releases

**R03. The modules' folders lie in `modules/<name>/`, where `<name>` is the second half of the module ID `novatrail/<name>`: `olympiad-math`, `grade-3-math` and `english-essays`. CI checks all the modules on every pull request and compares each with its newest published version — by the index of the newest release and that version's package. A release builds the packages of all the modules, and carries the cumulative index and only the versions that it publishes itself — as an archive `sha256-<hex>.tar.gz` per package. The withdrawals lie in `withdrawals.yaml` at the root. Item 2.1 of [architecture.md](architecture.md) is decided.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 while T01 was planned (RUN.md, T01, «Решено автором»): to four questions — where the folders are, what the modules are called, how the packages lie in a release and what CI compares with — by the recommendations, and the rest by the plan that the author approved. The code of all this — CI, `release.yml` and the recipe `release-files` — comes with the content epic (row 3.6 of [COORDINATION.md](../spec/COORDINATION.md)); only `.gitattributes` is written now.
- **The repository's tree:**
  - the module's folder is `modules/<name>/`, and `<name>` is the second half of its ID: the module `novatrail/olympiad-math` lies in `modules/olympiad-math/`. Inside is the module layout by the format ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10; R46 spec). Where the folder lies and what it is called the format left to `modules` (ibid.);
  - in `modules/` — only the modules' folders: neither other files nor files and folders starting with `.`. Each module in `modules/` on `main` comes out in a release — as its package, and the eval set does not go into the package (R62 spec). A module's draft lives in a branch;
  - the names: `olympiad-math` — olympiad maths 1–6, `grade-3-math` — school maths for grade 3 on a workbook, `english-essays` — short essays in English (R41 spec). The IDs of these modules are `novatrail/olympiad-math`, `novatrail/grade-3-math` and `novatrail/english-essays`: kebab-case starting with a letter, as the form `module_id` requires (R46 spec). The name names the subject and the audience, not the explanation language: the explanation languages of the slice are О-2, and it is open. A module in another explanation language is another module ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10), and its name will get a suffix with a BCP 47 tag, for example `olympiad-math-en`;
  - at the root — the list of withdrawals `withdrawals.yaml`, from the first withdrawal on (below), the README and the `LICENSE` of the repository (Д22 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`). The folder of each module has its own `LICENSE` too (the 2026-10-28 amendment to R46 spec);
  - `.gitattributes` is `modules/** -text`: git converts the line endings of no file of the modules, neither at a commit nor at a checkout. The package carries `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and the media files byte for byte, and a conversion to CR LF would change the package digest (Д44 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`; [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10). `spec` protects `format/` the same way — `format/** -text`.
- **CI:**
  - every pull request and push to `main` checks all the modules, not only the changed ones. The CLI of `module-kit` checks each folder `modules/*/`. CI checks the tree — in `modules/` only the modules' folders, and the ID of each is `novatrail/<folder>` — and builds the package of each module. So a new tag of `spec` or of `module-kit` re-checks all the modules without a special rule;
  - **the CLI is pinned in one place** — in the `toolchain` stage of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`: the release binary of `module-kit` at an exact tag and sha256 sum (R34 spec). The same binary is taken both by CI and by the recipe `release-files` on a release (below), so CI checks the modules with the same version of the CLI that the release will build them with. The binary's signature is checked by the recipe `release-verify` in CI of the pull request that changes the tag and the sum, before the first run. From then on the sum holds the binary (R77 spec; RULES.md, Hard rules). This difference of the container from the container of `spec` will be recorded as a decision when it comes with the code (CLAUDE.md, Environment);
  - **the comparison with what is published.** CI takes the `module-index.json` of the newest release of `modules` and checks its signature with the recipe `release-verify`. For each module:
    - if its version is already in the index, the digest of the package that CI built matches the digest of the entry. Otherwise the content was edited without raising the version, and the release would refuse by `module-index.one-package` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 26) already after the merge;
    - if the version is new, it is above each published version of the module, withdrawn ones too: a version is published once, and one line of versions lives in a module's folder. The CLI of `module-kit` compares the module by the rules of semver (architecture 3.7 spec) with the package of the newest of them. CI downloads the package from the release that its entry names and checks it by the chain of digests from the checked index (R77 spec);
    - if the previous version is written in a format version that the CLI no longer reads — and while the format is v0, this happens after each of its transitions (R44 spec), — the CLI checks only that the version has grown. Which version part a transition raises is decided by the rules of semver of `module-kit` (R44 spec);
    - before the first release there is no index, and all the versions are new;
  - **the gate of `sim`** — from the first tag of `sim` (R76 spec; Т-16). A module's graph has changed if the canonical text of the member `skill_graph` of its server part differs from the same member of the newest published version of the same format version. A new module's graph has changed. A pull request that changes the tag of `sim` runs the simulator on the graphs of all the modules. The simulator runs on the package that CI built: `sim` reads the server part by the types of the canonical view, without `module-kit` (architecture 3.9 spec);
  - **a format transition goes without `sim`.** If the previous version of a module is written in another format version, the simulator is not run in this pull request. `sim` at its former tag will not read the package of the new format version, and the graph is not changed in such a pull request: it is changed by a separate pull request when `sim` moves to a new tag (repositories 4 spec). That the graph has not changed in it is held by review, not by CI: the canonical text will not compare the member `skill_graph` of different format versions;
  - the gate is the graph, as card 3.10 spec, repositories 4 spec and Т-16 require. But `sim` and `engine` read the tasks too from the canonical view (Д44 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`), and a change of the tasks' bindings does not set off the gate. Whether it should is В5 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»;
  - what to run is decided inside the job, not by a filter `paths` on the workflow: the checks of a workflow that a filter skipped hang "Pending", and a required one holds the merge ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4);
  - which modules the eval runner of `tutor` runs — from stage 2 (R24 spec) — will be decided by T02 and by the task that connects it: the eval set does not go into the package (R62 spec), and the comparison of packages does not see its changes.
- **The release:**
  - `release.yml` is by the template of architecture 3.3 .github, with the input `previous-file: module-index.json` from the first release. The shared `release.yml` has this input since v0.1.14 ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4);
  - the recipe `release-files VERSION PREVIOUS` runs in the container of the `toolchain` stage, on the commit's tree without git, without submodules and without the network. The CLI of `module-kit` in it is the same binary from the `toolchain` stage as CI's (above);
  - the recipe builds the packages of all the modules and the index: from the previous index, checked by the shared release, its own packages, the tag VERSION and `withdrawals.yaml` (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R63 spec). For the first release the shared release gives an empty string instead of a path, and the recipe tells the build that there is no previous index. One may build all the modules or only the changed ones (R63 spec), but the recipe cannot even learn which have changed: there is no git in its tree. And building all of them on a release catches once more an edit without raising the version, with the refusal `module-index.one-package`;
  - into `dist/` the recipe puts:
    - `module-index.json`;
    - `LICENSE` — the text of Apache-2.0 from the root, as in a release of `module-kit`;
    - an archive for each package whose entry names the tag VERSION — that is, only for the versions that this release publishes. The package of a version that the index already named lies in the release that its entry names, and does not come out a second time.

    The bundle `.sigstore.json` for each file is put by the shared release. A release that only withdraws versions carries the index and `LICENSE`;
  - **the archive of a package** is named by the package digest, with `:` replaced by `-`, with `.tar.gz`: `sha256-<64 hexadecimal digits>.tar.gz`. Inside is the package as files, at the root of the archive, without a top folder: `inventory.json`, `server.json`, `device.json` and `device/` with `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and `media/<path>`. Apart from these files and their folders, there is nothing in the archive — neither links nor other entries. The archive's address is derived from the index's entry — the tag `release` and `package_digest`: `https://github.com/altnovatrail/modules/releases/download/<release>/sha256-<hex>.tar.gz`. It is read at the public address, without a token and without the REST API. Nothing holds the bytes of the archive itself, and nothing needs to: a reader checks the inventory against the package digest, the parts against the inventory, and the files against the list of the device part, and takes only the paths that this list names ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 25);
  - **the withdrawals** are `withdrawals.yaml`: YAML by the format's section 4, and the form is the member `withdrawals` of the index vector, `index-vector.schema.json`: module ID → version → withdrawal reason. The form requires at least one entry, so there is no file while nothing is withdrawn: then the recipe tells the build that there are no withdrawals. With the first withdrawal the file appears and from then on only grows: a repeat of a withdrawal with the same reason changes nothing (R63 spec);
  - **the fix of a withdrawn version.** If the newest version of a module is withdrawn, the fix — the next version of the same major — goes in the same pull request as the withdrawal, when it is ready. A withdrawal without a fix — when waiting for the fix is more dangerous: until it comes, the previous major becomes current (В6 in table 3 of the RUN.md of `api`). A version older than the newest cannot be fixed: a new version is above all the published ones (above), and a fix comes out only as a new version of the newest line;
  - **`paths`** — by the rule of the 2026-10-22 amendment to R31 spec: the list holds what the release is built from and what the consumer will get. The list itself is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3, and only there: a list written in two places diverges (the 2026-11-01 amendment to R31 spec). It holds the modules' folders without their `eval/`, `withdrawals.yaml`, `justfile`, the root `LICENSE` and `release.yml`, and not `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`.

    A trial on 2026-11-02, git 2.56.0, in a temporary repository. `git diff --name-only` with this list found a change of a module's `manifest.yaml`, task, lesson, `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and `modules/a/media/eval/p.png`, of `withdrawals.yaml`, `justfile`, the root `LICENSE` and `release.yml`. It did not find a change of a module's `eval/`, of `docs/`, `RUN.md`, `README.md`, `ci.yml` and `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, and `git diff --quiet` after a commit with only them returned 0 — no release. The magic `glob` is needed because without it `*` in a pathspec matches `/` too (the 2026-11-01 amendment to R31 spec): `modules/*/eval/*` would exclude a module's `media/eval/` too;
  - **the tag that CI of `canvas` takes** is the `vX.Y.Z` of a release of `modules`: the number of the repository itself, not a module's version (R31 spec; R63 spec). CI of `canvas` reads the index of this release at an exact tag and takes the packages from the releases that its entries name. The index of the newest release it reads only for the withdrawal marks (the 2026-10-28 amendment to R25 spec);
  - releases are not deleted or rewritten: immutable releases are a condition of the signature check (R77 spec; Д3 in table 4 of RUN.md), and a verdict is repeated on old packages (architecture 3.10 spec).

— *Why:*
- **`modules/<name>/`**: the root holds the service files, and the rule "a folder in `modules/` is a module" is checked without exceptions. The format's index vectors lay out a release's modules the same way — `modules/<name>/` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 9). There is no publisher in the path: only the official modules lie here, and third-party ones will come with the registry service (R17 spec; the recommendation of О-9).
- **The names**: an ID is forever, since the full IDs of skills and tasks — `novatrail/olympiad-math/skill/…` — are kept by the learner model and the progress ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10). A language in the name would decide О-2 before it is itself decided.
- **The archive by digest**: the name is derived from the index's entry — each has a digest, no replacement of `/` is needed, and two names will not collide. The build of the image of `api` needs only the packages of the window, and it wants to take them by the entry, without going through the release's files (В6 in table 3 of the RUN.md of `api`). The release files are a flat list with safe names, and the media paths have `/`: a package as loose files would not fit into a release without a renaming of its own, and a release has no more than 1000 files ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4).
- **Only the new versions**: an entry names one release (R63 spec), and no entry would name the copies of a package in later releases.
- **The comparison with what is published**: CI catches before the merge exactly what the release would later refuse on, and takes the previous version where the consumers take it — from immutable releases (architecture 3.10 spec). The graph is compared by the canonical view, not by the file's text, so a change of a comment in `skill-graph.yaml` does not run the simulator. A format transition goes without `sim`, as repositories 4 spec requires.
- **All the modules on every pull request**: there are three of them, and a rule "which one has changed" would save nothing for the check. A new tag of a tool requires checking them all anyway.
- **One binary of the CLI for CI and the release**: two places where the version is pinned diverge, and then CI would let through modules that the release will reject after the merge.
- **The Dockerfile is not in `paths`**: the package and the index depend only on the files of the module and the format version of its module manifest ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 25), not on the version of `module-kit`. Raising the CLI would give a release with the same index, and the Dockerfile changes every week: Dependabot proposes the base image (`.github/dependabot.yml`, `docker`, `weekly`). A new format version changes the modules' manifests, and they are in the list. The price is the whole `justfile` in the list, though it also holds the CI recipes: a change of them will give a release with the same index, as for the kind go. This is a rare change, and the recipe `release-files` is in the `justfile` too.
- **`-text` on the whole of `modules/`**: the package digest is taken of `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and the media files byte for byte.

— *Rejected:*
- the modules' folders at the root, `<name>/`: the root would mix the modules with the service folders, and the rule "a folder is a module" would need exceptions;
- `modules/novatrail/<name>/`: an extra level for the sake of publishers that will not be here (О-9);
- the names `school-math-3` and `short-essays`: the second does not say that the essays are in English;
- the names `school-math-grade-3` and `essays-in-english`: each full ID is longer without new clarity;
- names with the explanation language, `olympiad-math-ru`: they would decide О-2 before it is itself decided;
- an archive by ID and version, `novatrail+olympiad-math+1.2.0.tar.gz`: the name is held by a replacement rule, not by the content;
- the packages of all the current versions in each release: copies that no entry names;
- a package as loose files in a release — the argument above;
- one archive for all the packages of a release: the build of the image of `api` takes only the packages of the window (В6 in table 3 of the RUN.md of `api`);
- a comparison with the pull request's base in git: an edit of a published version would be caught only by the release's refusal, after the merge, and "the graph has changed" would mean an edit of the text of `skill-graph.yaml`, of a comment too;
- a comparison with the module's folder at the release's tag in git: CI would need the whole history, and the link of the folder with the published package would have to be checked by a rebuild;
- checking only the changed modules in CI — the argument above;
- a filter `paths` on the CI workflow: a required check would hang "Pending";
- the withdrawals in the module's folder: such a file would break `module.layout`, and a module does not see a file starting with `.` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10);
- `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` in `paths`, as for the kind go: raising `module-kit` and a new base image would give releases equal to the previous one;
- the CLI in CI at a tag of its own, and in the recipe from the Dockerfile: two versions of the CLI could diverge — the argument above;
- comparing `skill_graph` in a format transition too: the member of different format versions would be compared as text, and a transition that changes the graph's form would run `sim` at its former tag, and that one will not read the package of the new version;
- `text eol=lf` instead of `-text`: the attribute `text` turns on the conversion of line endings for the media files too, and the PNG signature has the bytes `0D 0A`. On `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` alone it would work, but `-text` holds everything byte for byte, as in `spec`.

— *Consequence:*
- [architecture.md](architecture.md): subsection 2.1 is decided, how it is built — sections 3.1–3.3, the sources — section 4. `.gitattributes` is written now, before the first module;
- four rows go «в реестр spec» into table 3 of RUN.md:
  - В2 — the list of `paths` of the kind `modules` in the template of architecture 3.3 .github;
  - В3 — what CI and the release of `modules` expect from the CLI of `module-kit` and from the format;
  - В4 — the layout of the packages in the releases of `modules` as the answer to В6 `api`, for `api`, `canvas`, `sim`, `tutor` and card 3.10 spec;
  - В5 — whether to run `sim` on a change of the tasks' bindings too, not only of the graph;
- Д3 in table 4 of RUN.md names the shared `release.yml` no lower than v0.1.14: the input `previous-file` requires this;
- CI, `release.yml`, the recipe `release-files` and the CLI in the `toolchain` stage are written by the content epic, which T04 will propose.

— RUN.md, T01; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.1, 3.1–3.3 and 4; `.gitattributes`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* **the fix of a withdrawn version and the version comparison — as `spec` v0.6.5 decided them (R06).** The second 2026-11-02 amendment to R63 spec and the amendment of the same day to R49 spec speak of them otherwise than R03 above. The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in T08 (RUN.md, T08), both by the recommendations.
- **The fix of a withdrawn version.** A withdrawn newest version of a module is fixed by a version above it by semver, and which part to raise the rules of semver decide. A change of a published task's key is major (concepts 3 spec), so the fix of a version withdrawn by `wrong_key` is the next major. A fix that is ready goes in the same pull request as the withdrawal and comes out in the same release: a version that comes out in this release can be withdrawn too (R63 spec). The withdrawal does not wait for the fix. The item "the fix of a withdrawn version" above reads so: "the next version of the same major" and "a withdrawal without a fix — when waiting for the fix is more dangerous" no longer apply.
- **The version comparison.** The CLI of `module-kit` compares the new version of a module by the rules of semver with each published version of the module, withdrawn ones too (architecture 3.7 spec; the 2026-11-02 amendment to R49 spec). The new version is above each published one, so these are all the published versions of the module. CI downloads the package of each from the release that its entry names and checks it by the chain of digests from the checked index. A version written in a format version that the CLI no longer reads it does not compare, but only checks that the new version is above it. Its skills are then held by the transition rule above — the graph is not changed in a format transition, and review holds this, — and CI compares the next version with the version of the transition. The item "the comparison with what is published" above reads so: instead of "with the package of the newest of them" — with the package of each. The gate of `sim` still compares the graph with the newest published version of the same format version: it asks whether the graph has changed, not whether semver is broken.
- *Rejected:*
  - "the next version of the same major", as it was above: a change of the key is major, and `spec` rejected this option (the second 2026-11-02 amendment to R63 spec);
  - R03 as it was and a row «В реестр spec» against the decision of `spec`: there are no new facts;
  - a comparison only with the newest published one: one line of versions and a comparison on every pull request give the same by induction, but only while each published version has itself passed the comparison, and card 3.7 spec requires comparing with each. The price of the chosen option is the archive of each published version of the module in each pull request that raises this module's version: a version that is already in the index CI only checks by its digest.

— RUN.md, T08 and В3; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 3.2 and 3.3; R06.

— *Amendment 2026-11-06:* **the comparison across a format transition, the form of the withdrawals, the list of `paths` and the form of the CLI's call — as `spec` v0.9.3 recorded them (R09).** The answers of `spec` to В2 and В3 (Д80 and Д84 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`) and the format 0.3 (R89, R101 spec).
- **Across a format transition.** The package of a version written in another format version `diff` reads by the places that the format keeps in all versions: in the inventory, in the names of the package's files and in the canonical view — the languages, the bands, the skills and `retired_skills`, the keys of the tasks, lessons, traps and techniques ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 2 and 27). The rules about skills, `retired_skills` and IDs go across a transition too, and the version part, while the format is v0, is major. Which rules compare with each published version and which with the newest, the format says (section 27 ibid.). CI gives `diff` the packages of all the published versions of the module, withdrawn ones too, and across a format transition too (Д107 `spec`). The item "the comparison with what is published" above and the 2026-11-02 amendment read so: "the CLI checks only that the version has grown", "the skills are held by the transition rule, and review holds this" and "CI compares the next version with the version of the transition" no longer apply. The gate of `sim` is still not run across a format transition: `sim` at its former tag will not read the package of the new version.
- **The form of the withdrawals** is `$defs/withdrawals` of the index's schema, `module-index.schema.json`, to which the member `withdrawals` of the index vector refers too (T67 `spec`; [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 26). The item "the withdrawals" above reads so: instead of "the member `withdrawals` of the index vector, `index-vector.schema.json`" — `$defs/withdrawals` of the index's schema.
- **The list of `paths`** is kept by the template of architecture 3.3 .github, with the reason why it has no `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, and [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3, refers to it (the 2026-11-01 amendment to R31 spec; the answer to В2). On `main` of `.github` — commit af40f94, read 2026-11-06 — the template has no kind `modules` yet: `.github` brings it in with its move to a tag (Д80 `spec`). Until then the list of section 3.3 is the one that the template will get. The `release.yml` of epic E2 takes the list from the template if the template already carries it, and where they diverge, the template is right. The item "`paths`" above reads so: "and only there" no longer applies.
- **The form of the call** of the CLI's commands `index` and `diff` is architecture 3.6 module-kit: the recipes of `modules` cite it and check the call with their tests (R102 spec; Д108 `spec`).
- *Rejected:*
  - the comparison across a format transition only by the growth of the version and by review, as it was: R89 spec rejected it — the rule "no entry has gone" holds only the skills, and the IDs of tasks, lessons, traps and techniques have no table;
  - the list of `paths` only in section 3.3, against the 2026-11-01 amendment to R31 spec: there are no new facts.

— R09; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.3.

— *Second amendment 2026-11-06:* **CI checks the signature of the binary of `module-kit` on every pull request and push to `main`, not only on the pull request that changes the tag and the sum.** The author's answer while T09 was planned (R10). The item "the CLI is pinned in one place" above reads so: instead of "in CI of the pull request that changes the tag and the sum, before the first run" — "in CI of each pull request and push to `main`, before CI checks the modules with it". The first run of the binary is `module-kit --version` at the build of the image, and before it the signature is held by the chain that R10 names: `SHA256SUMS`, checked by `release-verify` before the sums are written into `ARG`, and the sum, which the build checks before the run. From then on the sum still holds the binary. The rest of the item is as it was, and how the CLI stands in the `toolchain` stage and how CI calls it R10 recorded.
- *Rejected:* a check only when the tag and the sum change, as it was — the argument is in R10.

— R10; RUN.md, T09; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07:* **the gate of `sim` is by R103 spec, not by the graph.** `spec` v0.9.5 closed Т-16 (R18; Д111 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`). The items "the gate of `sim`", "a format transition goes without `sim`" and "the gate is the graph" above read so:
- **the gate of `sim`** — from the first tag of `sim` (R76 spec). CI of `modules` runs `sim` when what the simulator reads from the package has changed — whether it has changed is decided by `sim` itself with its command "has it changed", for a new module too, — on the pull request's package that CI built, with invariants and absolute thresholds, without bands. A pull request that changes the tag of `sim` runs the simulator on all the modules. `sim` reads the server part by the types of the canonical view, without `module-kit` (architecture 3.9 spec);
- **a format transition goes without `sim`**, as it was. That nothing of what the simulator reads — not only the graph — has changed in a transition is held by review, and the simulator will see the transition's package with the pull request that raises the tag of `sim` (В23 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»);
- **"the gate is the graph"** and the definition "the graph has changed" by the canonical text of the member `skill_graph` no longer apply. Nor does the comparison of the graph with the newest published version of the same format version, which the 2026-11-02 amendment left to the gate of `sim`.

*Why:* a gate by the graph alone would let through a pull request that reweighs the tasks or changes their bindings without touching the graph: `sim` and `engine` read the tasks too (Д44 `spec`; R103 spec). Without bands — the pull request's package has no base to compare them with. What "has changed" is decided by `sim`: it knows what it reads, and a copy of this rule in `modules` would diverge from it.

*Rejected:*
- a gate by the graph, as it was — the argument above; R103 spec rejected it too;
- an own check "has it changed" in CI of `modules` by the canonical view — a second place for the rule of `sim`;
- the graph and the tasks' bindings without the rest of what the simulator reads — a boundary that `modules` would draw for `sim`.

— R18; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2 and 3.2; RUN.md, Д5 and В23.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08:* **the review of a format transition — by the 2026-11-07 amendment to R103 spec, the answer to В23.** `spec` v0.9.8 recorded it (R19): in a format transition `modules` changes nothing of what the simulator reads from the package — neither the graph nor the tasks' bindings — except the writing itself in the new format version, and the review of `modules` holds this. All of that is changed by a separate pull request after step 4 of repositories 4 spec, with the simulator. The item "a format transition goes without `sim`" of the 2026-11-07 amendment reads so: instead of "and the simulator will see the transition's package with the pull request that raises the tag of `sim` (В23 …)" — "and this is changed by a separate pull request after step 4 of repositories 4 spec, with the simulator".

*Why:* review held only the graph, and a pull request of a transition that reweighs the tasks or changes their bindings without touching the graph would come out in a release without the simulator — the very case that R103 spec named as the argument for a gate wider than the graph. The change is one phrase in repositories 4 spec, and `modules` already wrote a review wider than the graph (the 2026-11-07 amendment).

*Rejected* (together with `spec`, by the recommendation of В23): the transition waits for a tag of `sim` that reads the new format version and goes with the simulator — each format transition would stand still until that tag; review holds only the graph, as it was — the argument above.

— R19; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14 (T69), by the author's answers in the planning of T69:* **the fourth folder is `modules/check-board/`, the module `novatrail/check-board` of the check board of `tutor` (R33). Its name names the board whose tasks it holds, not the subject and the audience: the rule of the names is for the modules of the slice, and this module stands beside them, not among them (the 2026-11-14 amendment to R76 spec).** It is in Russian and has no twin in another explanation language, so its name has no suffix of a language. CI and the release take the folder as they take every folder of `modules/` (R17). — RUN.md, T69; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 3.10.

## Samples in the eval set

**R04. An adult volunteer's sample is added to the eval set by the eval-set organizer — a maintainer of `modules`. The organizer accepts the signed consent form, gets the file, checks it by hand before the first push and brings a pull request from a branch of the repository itself. The organizer signs off the commit with their own name under clause (c) of the DCO, and the volunteer certifies clause (a) in the form. The form lies outside the repositories, and nothing of it is in the repository. That the volunteer is an adult is said by their declaration in the form. A module's author adds their own sample themselves, with their own DCO sign-off. Item 2.2 of [architecture.md](architecture.md) is decided.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in planning T02 (RUN.md, T02, «Решено автором»). Four questions — who adds a sample, where the consent lies, how to check that the volunteer is an adult, and how authors add samples — were decided by the recommendations, and the rest by the plan that the author approved. There is no code. The checks of CI will come with the eval protocol (epic E3 in the RUN.md of `spec`), and the eval runner from stage 2. There will be no volunteers' samples until epic E4 in the RUN.md of `spec` approves the texts of consent: until then the samples page does not open the purpose «eval-набор» ("eval set") (R11 canvas).
- **The path** — step by step in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.4, and only there. Its rules:
  - the organizer accepts the form before the first sample (R11 canvas; П-18), gets the file by the channel that the form will name, and checks it before the file gets into any branch. The channel is not the platform's server (architecture 3.2 spec) and not GitHub;
  - a file that did not pass the check is not edited and not added: the volunteer writes the sample anew;
  - in git and on GitHub — only the organizer. The organizer is the commit's author and its sign-off, and neither `Co-authored-by` nor the volunteer's data are in the commit's message, the branch's name or the pull request's text. The volunteer needs no GitHub account;
  - the samples of several volunteers, if there are any, go in one commit: the commit does not show which files come from one person;
  - a pull request with a sample from a volunteer or from a fork is not merged.
- **DCO.** Clause (c) is "The contribution was provided directly to me by some other person who certified (a), (b) or (c) and I have not modified it" ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4). So the form carries the text of DCO 1.1, and the volunteer certifies clause (a) in it. The organizer gives the file its name and does not change the content: clause (c) holds while the file is byte for byte the one the volunteer certified. The DCO check checks the sign-off against the commit's author (section 4 ibid.), so the commit's author is the one who signed off.
- **The consent** is the signed form, and it lies outside the repositories, with the organizer. Agents are not let near it, as they are not let near eval samples outside the repositories (П-12). The link with the samples goes from its side: the record of the form names the SHA-256 digests of the files added under it. In the repository there is nothing of the form — neither a name, nor a number, nor a code. What else is in the form, where the forms and their records lie and how long they are kept is decided by epic E4 in the RUN.md of `spec` together with П-16 (В6 in table 3 of RUN.md).
- **An adult** — by the declaration "I am 18 or older" in the signed form. The volunteer makes the same declaration on the samples page (R11 canvas; П-6). Whether the declaration is enough in every country, П-18 asks the lawyer.
- **An author's sample** passes the same checks, but without a form and without an organizer. An author with write access adds their sample themselves, with their own DCO sign-off under clause (a), as the author writes their measurement samples without a form (R11 canvas). An author without write access adds their samples as a volunteer.
- **The checks** — by the hands of whoever adds the sample, before the first push, without AI and without agents. After the eval protocol CI repeats the fields and the schema, without a key. The list is [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.4.
- **The eval runner in CI** — from stage 2 (R24 spec), only on a pull request:
  - runs the modules whose `eval/` has changed against the pull request's base or whose package has changed against the published one (R03), new ones among them;
  - runs all the modules when a pull request changes the tag of `tutor`. A move to a new version of the format changes the packages of all modules, but the same pull request also changes the tag of `tutor` (repositories 4 spec);
  - does not run on a push to `main`. The price of a run is named before the first launch (R24 spec).

— *Why:*
- **The organizer adds it.**
  - A push publishes. A branch of a public repository is seen at once, and after a rewritten history the commits remain "Through any pull requests that reference them" ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4). A name on a sample must be found before publication, not at the review of the pull request.
  - The DCO sign-off and the pull request's author tie the sample to an account forever: clause (d) of the DCO, and the merge only by a merge commit (R22, R30 spec).
  - A GitHub account is allowed from age 13 and does not say whether its owner is an adult. The form would have to be tied to the account.
  - On a pull request from a branch of the repository the eval job with a key does its work, and on a fork it fails, and a maintainer moves the change anyway (R24 spec).
  - The organizer accepts the form (R11 canvas), so everything that knows the volunteer's name stays with one person.
- **No trace in the repository.** A number of the form on a sample would tie together the published samples of one person, and it is for this that R09 canvas removed the writer code from the eval-set sample. The digest in the record of the form gives the same link, but only to whoever has the form. A commit that holds the samples of one person and only them would tie them the same way, so the samples of several volunteers go in one commit.
- **The declaration**: so П-6 advises, so R11 canvas decided. A document with the date of birth is extra data, and nothing can be recorded from it anyway.
- **An author's sample without a form**: the sign-off under clauses (a) and (d) of the DCO says the same as the form — the right to give the sample and publication forever — and a form to oneself adds nothing.
- **By hand, without AI and without agents**: before the check a sample may carry a name. An agent is not given eval samples outside the repositories (П-12), and the form allows a run at an AI provider only for a published sample, in CI.
- **Not editing a sample with a name**: erased strokes change the sample on which the markup stands, and take it out of clause (c).
- **The runner's rule**: an eval set has no published form — it is outside the package (R62 spec) — so only git sees an edit of it. An edit of the package changes the verdict by which false alarms are counted (architecture 4.6 spec), and a new tag of `tutor` changes the prompts, the models and the runner. A branch is up to date before the merge (R30 spec), so the same tree that the pull request ran lands on `main`.

— *Rejected:*
- the volunteer opens the pull request themselves, from their own account: the sample is published before the check, the tie to the account stays forever, and the account says nothing about age — the arguments above;
- both ways: each would keep its own drawbacks, and there would be two checks;
- a random number of the form on the sample in git — the argument above;
- the declaration and a document that the organizer looks at — the argument above;
- the authors' samples by a form, like the volunteers' — a form to oneself;
- looking for a name on a sample with AI or an agent, and checking the sample only at the review of the pull request — the arguments above;
- the eval runner on all modules on every pull request and on a push to `main`: paid runs that change nothing.

— *Consequence:*
- [architecture.md](architecture.md): subsection 2.2 is decided, how it is built — section 3.4, the runner's rule — in section 3.2, the sources — in section 4;
- four rows go «в реестр spec» into table 3 of RUN.md:
  - В6 — what this path requires of the consent form, and the answers to the questions of П-18;
  - В7 — the premise of R25 spec, the samples page of `canvas` and the CONTRIBUTING of `.github`;
  - В8 — what the eval protocol must give the checks of `modules`;
  - В9 — where a model's admission takes the eval sets of the published modules from;
- the checks of CI and the eval runner are written by the content epic or by the task that will connect the runner — when there is the eval protocol.

— RUN.md, T02; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2, 2.2, 3.2, 3.4 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-06:* **the sample file, its check without a key and the eval set are by the eval protocol of `spec` v0.9.3 (R09), and the layout of `eval/` is flat, `eval/<id>.json`.** The answers of `spec` to В6–В9 (Д80, Д94, Д95 and Д97 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`); R93–R95 spec; [contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), sections 11 and 13. The layout is the author's answer of 2026-11-06, by the recommendation (subsection 2.4 of [architecture.md](architecture.md)).
- **The sample file** is the message `Sample` of the package `novatrail.eval.v1`: one JSON per sample, without the task's key, with a license for an eval-set sample and with the markup `markup`. A reader reads it strictly: an unknown field is a refusal. The PNG of the crops has only IHDR, IDAT and IEND, not one ancillary chunk (R93 spec). R04 expected "only the ancillary ones that the protocol names", and the table of checks of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.4, now says the same as R93 spec.
- **The check without a key** is the recipe of `spec` through the submodule, `just -f spec/justfile sample-check <files>`, in the `toolchain` stage: the same code that checks the examples of `spec`. `modules` writes no check of its own (R93 spec; T72 `spec`). CI runs the recipe on every sample file of the eval set, and on a pull request from a fork too. This is not the command of the eval runner of `tutor` that row В8 recommended: the rules of validity are one for the samples page, the runner and CI. The recipe checks each file separately and only by the rules of the sample file. The rules of the eval set — the purpose «eval-набор» ("eval set"), the markup, a task of its own module, not a child, IDs without repeats — it does not check ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 13), and until stage 2, until the eval runner, only the hands of whoever adds the sample hold them. Asking `spec` for a check of these rules without a key is row В16 of table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec».
- **A module's eval set** is each file under its `eval/` at any depth whose name ends in `.json`, except files and folders starting with `.`, and each is a sample of a task of its own module. The layout inside `eval/` the protocol left to `modules`.
- **The layout is flat:** a sample is a file `eval/<id>.json`, where `<id>` is the sample's field `id`, and in `eval/` there are neither folders nor other files, except files and folders starting with `.`. The organizer does not choose the name: the name is the sample's ID. CI checks the layout, and that the file's name is the ID of its sample, by a recipe of its own: this is a rule of the layout of `modules`, not a rule of the sample's validity, which `sample-check` holds. So two samples of a set do not share an ID either: one folder has no two files with one name.
  - *Why:* the protocol set the sample's ID "so that it can name the sample's file": for a person's sample it is random, and for a synthetic one it is derived from the seed, so the name says nothing about the person ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 11). The path repeats no other field of the file, and they have nothing to diverge from. The samples of a task whose open view a pull request changes are named by the runner's refusal.
  - *Rejected:* by task, `eval/<task id>/<id>.json` — the path would repeat the task's field and could diverge from it; by origin, `eval/synthetic/` and beside it — the path would repeat «кто писал» ("who wrote") and would divide people's samples into groups that are easier to match against one another.
- **A sample and an edit of the task.** The eval runner takes the task from the package that `module-kit` built on the pull request's commit, and rejects a sample whose digest of the task's open view did not match — then the run does not pass (R95 spec). So a pull request that changes a task's open view remakes or removes its samples in the same pull request. A volunteer's sample the organizer does not remake: clause (c) of the DCO holds while the file is byte for byte the one the volunteer certified. The organizer removes the sample or asks the volunteer to write it anew.
- **What else `spec` recorded:**
  - the eval-set organizer is a term of concepts 15 spec, and the argument of R25 spec "they open the pull request on GitHub anyway" is corrected (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R25 spec; В7);
  - what the form carries is П-16. Its text, where the forms and their records lie, how long they are kept, by which channel the file goes and what happens to a file that did not pass the check are «Форма добровольца eval-набора в E4» under table 2 of the RUN.md of `spec` (В6);
  - the admission of a new model: a pull request of `modules` moves to the tag of `tutor` with this model and runs the eval sets of all the modules, and `api` takes this tag only after its merge (repositories 4 spec; architecture 4.6 spec; В9). The runner's rule above already holds this.
- *Rejected:* a check of a sample's validity of its own in CI of `modules`, and a command of the runner of `tutor`: the rules of validity would be written a second time (R93 spec). The rules of the eval set that `sample-check` does not check `modules` does not write itself either, but asks `spec` for them by row В16: "MUST NOT write a check of its own" ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 11).

— R09; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.4 and 3.4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09:* **in `eval/` there is nothing starting with `.`, at any depth: neither a file nor a folder.** The layout above let in files and folders starting with `.`, because the protocol does not count them as part of the eval set ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 13). Decision 2 of epic E5 — the author's answer of 2026-11-09 in the breakdown, by the recommendation — rejected, after the guard too, a file `.json` with a name starting with `.` in its path. T47 widened it to one rule of the layout — by the executor, on the author's instruction (decision 3 of epic E5). It is held by `just eval-check` (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R25).
- *Why:* `sample-check` will not see a file starting with `.`, and the repository will publish it forever, like any other. A child sample under such a name — `eval/.drafts/x.json` — would land in git unchecked. One rule closes both `.json` and a file of any other kind, and there is no need to keep two rules in agreement. `.gitkeep` is not needed: git keeps no empty folders, and `eval/` appears with the first sample.
- *Rejected:* letting in files starting with `.`, as before — an unchecked sample in git; only a `.json` with a name starting with `.` in its path, as decision 2 said — a second rule on top of the layout, and a `.png` or a `.txt` under `eval/.drafts/` would pass.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11 (T61):* **the author adds their own sample under their own form: they sign the same form of the eval-set volunteer and accept it themselves, as the organizer. The order is step by step, in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.4, "The path of an author's sample". `.claude/settings.json` forbids a session of Claude Code in this repository to read `modules/*/eval/*`, except `synthetic-*.json`, and `just eval-match` rejects a person's sample with an ID starting with `synthetic-`.** The items "An author's sample" and "An author's sample without a form" above — "without a form and without an organizer", "a form to oneself adds nothing" — read so: there is a form, and the organizer is the author themselves. The rest in them is as it was: the same checks, the sign-off under clause (a) of the DCO, an author without write access — as a volunteer.
- **Why a form.** The samples page of `canvas` for the purpose «eval-набор» ("eval set") requires two declarations: «мне 18 лет или больше…» and «форма согласия версии … подписана и принята организатором» ("the consent form of version … is signed and accepted by the organizer"). A declaration of the author, like «я автор проекта и пишу свои образцы» ("I am the project's author and write my own samples") on the measurement draft, this purpose does not have (architecture 3.6 canvas, read 2026-11-11 on `main` of `canvas`). The author's answer in T61, 2026-11-11, by the recommendation. *Rejected:* a request to `canvas` for an author's declaration for the eval set — the author's samples would wait for one more task of `canvas`, and the form gives the author's samples the same proof of the license and of clause (a) of the DCO as a volunteer's.
- **The ban on reading.** Before the check a person's sample may carry a name, and neither AI nor an agent looks at it (П-12). The rule:

  ```json
  "deny": ["Read(modules/*/eval/*)", "Read(!modules/*/eval/synthetic-*.json)"]
  ```

  - Claude Code's path rules follow the gitignore syntax. A rule with `!` is a negation, and it cuts paths out of the `deny` rules of the same file before it; the order of the rules is deny, ask, allow, and allow makes no exception from deny (the documentation of Claude Code, [Configure permissions](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions.md), read 2026-11-11). A negation does not reach files inside a folder that a rule before it forbids whole, so a folder in `eval/` is closed entirely — the layout rejects it anyway.
  - The ban holds for the tools for reading files and for the Bash commands that name the file — "`cat`, `head`, `tail`, `sed`, and `tee`" — and for redirections. But not for a program that opens files by itself, "like a Python or Node script", and not for `grep -r` without a file's name (ibid.). So a person's sample lies in the working copy only while the author adds it, and the session looks only at CI's output.
  - Checked in T61: in a session of Claude Code, `Read` of the stub file `modules/grade-3-math/eval/person-stub.json` — `{"id": "person-stub"}`, without anyone's data — was refused, "File is in a directory that is denied by your permission settings", and so were `cat` and `jq -r .id` with its name; `Read` of `synthetic-4.json` passed. The stub was removed.
- **The name.** The ban tells samples apart by name, and the name is the sample's ID (the layout above). A synthetic sample's ID is `synthetic-<seed>` (R110 spec), and a person's is random, and `canvas` does not name its shape. So `just eval-match` rejects a person's sample with an ID starting with `synthetic-`: a session could read such a file. The refusal is 1, the test is `test/eval-match.test.mjs`.
— [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.4; `.claude/settings.json`; `justfile`, the recipe `eval-match`; CLAUDE.md; RUN.md, T61.

## The port of standalone

**R05. The module `novatrail/olympiad-math` carries over standalone's olympiad maths by the table of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5. The topics become skills of the graph, and the traps, the techniques and Pólya's steps become catalogs, as in the format examples. Each of the 705 tasks becomes a task file with the same ID. The number answer in it is written by hand, and the wrong options become trap values. A lesson takes the explanations and examples from the site's topic page. standalone's content enters under MIT, and its notice stands in the module's `NOTICE`. Item 2.3 of [architecture.md](architecture.md) is decided, except the first batch: T07 decides it.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in planning T03 (RUN.md, T03, «Решено автором»). Four questions — whether to split the task, how the license enters, what to do with what the format does not express, and when to move to a new tag of `spec` — were decided by the recommendations, and the rest by the plan that the author approved. There is no code: the port is the code of the content epic and of row 3.7 of [COORDINATION.md](../spec/COORDINATION.md).
- **The source** is standalone at commit 89986bc, 2026-11-01. It has 17 topics, 20 traps, 705 tasks with solvers, 33 templates, 11 techniques of the site and 17 topic pages. The inventory is [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5. There are 705 tasks, not 603: the number 603 was before R309 standalone added the tasks of grades 5–6 (section 4 ibid.).
- **The catalogs and the graph** are taken from the format examples `olympiad-math`. The topics, their links and the traps in standalone have not changed since, and neither have the techniques (section 4 ibid.). The IDs — with `.` and `_` replaced by `-`. There are no collisions: the IDs of skills, traps and techniques are distinct within their kind.
- **A task** keeps its ID: all 705 IDs pass the format's ID pattern, are no longer than 13 characters and do not repeat. The task's topic becomes its primary skill, `grade_level` its band, `difficulty` its difficulty. The answer slot depends on the kind of the correct option:
  - an integer, a number with a unit or a fraction — an answer slot `anywhere` with the check `number` or `fraction`. The wrong options that are numbers become trap values — in the check kind's notation and without a unit — with their own trap and explanation. The trap of a non-numeric wrong option goes into the task's `traps`;
  - everything else — a word, a time on a clock, a date, a percentage, "yes" and "no" and any other text — an answer slot `choice` with five options, and the wrong options become trap values;
  - a task whose question points to the options — "Which of these numbers…" — also gets `choice`, though its answer is a number: without the options the question is not posed.
- **An explanation that names the correct answer is rewritten**, or else `task.no-leaks` will find it. A key that also stands in the statement the check does not look for. In tasks with an integer answer there are 159 such explanations, in 119 tasks, and with a fraction or a number with a unit 5 more (a count of 2026-11-02). If for word answers T07 chooses a choice by hand, the check will also look for the words of the correct option in the explanations, and T07 will count them.
- **The task's solver** does not go into the module: the key of a fixed task is the author's (concepts 9.4 spec). The port runs it once and checks the key and the trap values against it. The templates go to the rest of the port, rewritten for the dialect of R61 spec.
- **What the format does not express** is in the table of section 3.5, with the fate of each point. There are no rows «в реестр spec» about the format yet. No point hinders the port. The first batch may need the author's hint: every task of band 5–6 has one. Whether the batch takes such tasks without the hint or asks for the field earlier, T07 will decide. The other requests to 0.y will be gathered by T38 `spec` after the first content (Д60 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`).
- **The license.**
  - standalone's content is under MIT. The module's `NOTICE` carries the text of MIT with "Copyright (c) 2026 MathTrail" and a line saying what is taken from standalone (R12 spec; the 2026-10-28 amendment to R46 spec).
  - 442 tasks of grades 1–4 standalone took word for word from its prototype, and the format example takes Pólya's steps from the prototype's coach. The prototype is under Apache-2.0, without `NOTICE` and without a filled-in copyright line. Who the copyright holder is neither of the two repositories names: both have one and the same author on GitHub, and standalone's copyright line is "MathTrail".
  - So the module does not rest on there being one author. `NOTICE` names the prototype too, and that its content is changed here — by section 4(b) of Apache-2.0: "You must cause any modified files to carry prominent notices stating that You changed the files". The copy of Apache-2.0 is carried by the module's `LICENSE` — section 4(a), and a `NOTICE`, which section 4(d) would have it keep, the prototype does not have.
- **Provenance.** standalone's wording is its own, and the ideas are taken from open sources (section 4 ibid.). A task has no field of provenance (concepts 9.4 spec), and what this means for О-10 is В11 in table 3 of RUN.md.
- **The task is split.** The first batch — the rule of choice, the tasks, the slot bindings, the lessons, the language and the pictures — goes into a new task, T07. Before it comes T08 — the move to `spec` v0.6.5. T03 runs at v0.6.3: the format at both tags is 0.2.

— *Why:*
- **`NOTICE`, not a re-release**: neither an act of the copyright holder is needed nor an answer to who "MathTrail" in the copyright line is. The price is one file in the device part. MIT is compatible with Apache-2.0 (R12 spec).
- **The same ID**: it already fits and keeps the trace to standalone's task and its solver. A local ID is forever ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1).
- **A number answer — by hand**: R76 spec requires handwriting from the first day and rejected "only a choice of an option". With a choice of five the learner guesses with a probability of 1/5 (Д32 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`), and with a handwritten number there is no guessing. A trap value catches the same error as the letter of a wrong option, and weighs as a verdict (concepts 9.4 spec).
- **A choice — for the rest**: the check `number` does not judge a word, and a time on a clock, a date and a quantity of several parts are neither a number nor a fraction. Format v0 has no such check kinds ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15). The question "Which of these…" without options is not posed.
- **`NOTICE` names the prototype too**: so section 4(b) of Apache-2.0 is met, whoever the copyright holder is. The price is one line.
- **The catalogs from the examples**: they are already checked against the format, and standalone has not changed them since.
- **The solver — not into the module**: in the format only a template has a solver, and a fixed task carries the author's key (R60, R61 spec).
- **The requests — later**: Д60 gathers the modules' requests after their first content, and what the format does not express does not hinder the first batch.
- **The split** — by rule 11 of RUN.md: the inventory, the table and the license are one check, and the first batch with the lessons another.

— *Rejected:*
- a re-release of the content under Apache-2.0 by its copyright holder — the argument above;
- new IDs by meaning: the trace to standalone would be lost;
- all answers a tapped choice, as Д32 `spec` recorded — the argument above;
- the tasks' solvers in the module: the format has nothing to hold them with;
- a row «в реестр spec» for each point that the format does not express: some of them will not be needed;
- T03 together with the first batch — by rule 11;
- the move to v0.6.5 before T03: the new tag gives the port nothing.

— *Consequence:*
- [architecture.md](architecture.md): subsection 2.3 is decided, except the first batch, how it is built — section 3.5, the sources — section 4;
- RUN.md:
  - new tasks T08 and T07, and T04 waits for T07 too;
  - two rows go «в реестр spec» into table 3: В10 — 705 tasks instead of 603 and a handwritten number instead of a choice, to Д32 `spec`; В11 — the provenance of the port, to О-10;
- the port's code — the converter of tasks and the drawing of pictures — is written by the content epic for the first batch and by row 3.7 for the rest.

— RUN.md, T03, T07, T08; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2, 2.3, 3.5 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08:* **the rest of the port takes standalone at commit 42b4408 of 2026-11-07, not at 89986bc. After 89986bc standalone added tasks that are solved from the picture, and E3 carries them over too. standalone now has 753 tasks, and E3 carries over 726 — all but the 27 of the first batch. The first batch stays at 89986bc.** The author's answer of 2026-11-08, before planning E3: «я добавил новые типы задач в standalone… Обнови E3, чтобы туда также попали все новые типы задач» ("I added new kinds of tasks to standalone… Update the epic so that all the new kinds of tasks get into it too"). There is no code.
- **What was added** (`git diff 89986bc 42b4408`, a count by jq, 2026-11-08; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5):
  - 48 tasks, each with a picture, a solution picture and a hint. 30 of them are in two new topics, `counting.figures` and `geometry.cubes_paper`, five per band. Another 18 are in five earlier topics, and each of them has the field `idea`;
  - the solver of each of the 48 tasks reads the picture: `solve(options, picture)`;
  - there are 39 templates, six of them new, and the solver of each of the six reads the picture too;
  - there are 17 kinds of pictures, the new ones `lattice`, `tiles`, `shapes` and `stairs`;
  - there are 19 topics: the two new ones have `builds_on` empty, and each has a page on the site.

  The traps and the techniques have not changed. Nor have all the 705 earlier tasks: the JSON of each at 42b4408 is the same as at 89986bc, the 27 tasks of the first batch included. The correct option of all 48 new tasks is an integer, there is no question "Which of these…" among them, and only one explanation names the key: that of `gaps-12-d2-6`, option 6 — "The steps were not divided by 3."
- **The batches of E3 name 42b4408, and `first.yaml` names 89986bc.** The provenance by R12 is checked by the commit of each batch. The solvers of the new tasks are run by the test bench of 42b4408: the same test `TestEveryReferenceTaskProvesItsOwnAnswer`, the same go.starlark.net 89a6a09411d5 and Go 1.27.3 in `go.mod`. The words of R12 "standalone is frozen, and its test bench at 89986bc will not change" are true of the test bench at 89986bc. standalone itself changed after that commit.
- **What E3 decides** beyond what has already been left to it:
  - how the `alt` of a task solved from the picture carries its data. Without the picture such a task cannot be solved: "Its facts stand on the picture alone, and without the picture it cannot be solved" (`content/instructions/task_writing_picture.md` of standalone). And the `alt` is read by a screen reader, and only it remains where the picture is not seen ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14);
  - what to do with the six templates whose solver reads the picture. The format's solver receives only the numbers of the open fields ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 24). The picture's path is `picture_path` without a place for a placeholder, so all the instances of a template have one picture.
- **«В реестр spec»** — В24 in table 3 of RUN.md: the card architecture 3.10 spec, repositories 7 spec, product spec and row 3.7 of [COORDINATION.md](../spec/COORDINATION.md) name 705 tasks at 89986bc and 33 templates.

— *Why:* so the author decided. The tasks solved from the picture are of the same module and the same traps, and 10 of the 48 are in the skills of the first batch, `counting-gaps` and `combinatorics-enumeration`. The earlier tasks at 42b4408 have not changed, so the new commit changes nothing in what E3 already had to carry over.

— *Rejected:* keeping E3 at 89986bc and carrying over the tasks solved from the picture later. They would come into the module separately, with one more clone and test bench, though the earlier tasks at 42b4408 have not changed.

— RUN.md, table 2 — E3, table 3 — В24; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2, 3.5, 3.7 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T18):* **not every answer that is not a number is a tapped choice.** A time on a clock `H:MM` and one word that stands in the question are a choice by hand, with the check's `options`, and a percentage is `number` with `units: ["%"]`. By tapping stay a word that is not in the question, a date, "yes" or "no", a quantity of several parts and other text, and the question "Which of these…". The words of the correct option in the explanations the converter looks for, at every choice: the format's check does not look for them (R20). — RUN.md, T18; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5.

## The tag `spec` v0.6.5

**R06. `modules` stands on `spec` v0.6.5, the latest release of `spec`. The tag brings the first batch showing the solution to a learner of the category `adult` from the start of the attempt and a hint on request (R82 spec), and the graph the chain rule (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R58 spec). The fix of a withdrawn version and version comparison it decided otherwise than R03, and R03 got an amendment. The rows «В реестр spec» of table 3 of RUN.md the session of `spec` now finds itself (R81 spec).**
- **What the tag decided for `modules`** (`git -C spec diff v0.6.3 v0.6.5`, read 2026-11-02; Д70–Д75 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`; [COORDINATION.md](../spec/COORDINATION.md), rows 3.4–3.7):
  - **the release and CI** — the answers of `spec` to В6–В9 `api` (Д70 `spec`; architecture 3.7 and 3.10 spec):
    - the name of a package's file in the release is derived from the index entry — without going through the release's files and without the REST API — and the name itself is decided by T01 `modules` (the second 2026-11-02 amendment to R63 spec). The recommendation of `spec` is an archive named by the hexadecimal part of `package_digest`, and "To decide when detailed" of card 3.10 spec repeats it too. The name of R03, `sha256-<hex>.tar.gz`, is the same hexadecimal part with the algorithm's name in front, and it stays: `modules` decides the name. So В4 of this repository is closed: to its question — to write the layout down in the card or to leave it a detail of `modules` — `spec` answered with a rule in the card and the name at `modules`. But whoever builds the address by the card's recommendation, not by R03, will not find the archive: the name and the two decided points of the card are carried «в реестр spec» by row В12 of table 3 of RUN.md;
    - a withdrawn newest version is fixed by a version above it by semver, not necessarily of the same major: an edit of the key is a major one (concepts 3 spec). A ready fix comes out in the same release as the withdrawal, and the withdrawal does not wait for the fix (ibid.). R03 said otherwise — the 2026-11-02 amendment to R03;
    - a new version of a module is compared with each published version below it by semver, the withdrawn ones too, and a withdrawal does not free the skills' IDs (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R49 spec). `modules` decides where CI takes the earlier versions from. R03 compared only with the newest — the 2026-11-02 amendment to R03;
    - "To decide when detailed" of card 3.10 spec names two more points — the name of a package's file and where CI takes a module's earlier versions from. Both are part of item 2.1 of [architecture.md](architecture.md), and R03 with its amendment decided them;
  - **the first batch** — an input of T07:
    - R82 spec (О-7 and О-21 closed; Д73 `spec`): the solution is shown by a button — to a learner of the category `adult` from the start of the attempt, to a child from the fourth wrong answer — and showing closes the attempt with the outcome "solution shown". The key and the task's solution go to the device only when the attempt is closed by a correct answer or by showing. The request "Hint" before the first wrong answer gives "givens and unknown" and the task's technique, after it the words of the current rung once more, and "explain differently" gives the same rung in other words. A rung speaks of the first "not yet" answer slot in order ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 19 — an editorial edit). The first application is tested by an adult (R76 spec), so they can open the solution of any task of the batch at once. A task with an item with a verdict requires a solution anyway (R57 spec);
    - the 2026-11-02 amendment to R58 spec (Д71 `spec`): a skill graph is a chain when the module has at least two skills and each stands in a tier of its own. An extra link does not break the chain, and an anchor skill gives no tier. The app derives the label "sequential" from `skill_tiers` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21). Whether the batch is sequential depends on its graph — T07 decides it;
    - the 2026-11-02 amendment to R69 spec (Д71 `spec`): "Step done" in format v0 does not read the work, and the exit condition of the last solving step leads nowhere, and the examples give it `none` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 18). The batch takes the solving steps from the format example (R05), so this changes nothing for it;
    - the 2026-11-02 addition to Д60 `spec`: the version 0.y from T38 `spec` will include an optional open field of a solving step, `description` — how to go by the step. This is a request of `app`, and while there is no field, `app` shows the step's name at the step;
  - **R81 spec — the inbox of `spec`.** The rows «В реестр spec» of table 3 of this RUN.md the session of `spec` finds itself, by the recipe `just inbox`: the rows on `main` have arrived, and the rows of open pull requests are "on the way". The author no longer carries them. The recipe recognizes the mark regardless of case and only at the start of the question — so В2–В11 open here. So say both RULES.md, Across repositories, and rule 2 of the template's RUN.md. Table 5 of the RUN.md of `spec` at v0.6.5 holds no rows of `modules`: none of В2–В11 is recorded yet;
  - **devcontainer** — the comments of the `toolchain` stage in the Dockerfile are true in a copy of any kind: Go is for the repository's code in Go, and in a copy without such code it is not used (Д75 `spec`, the answer to В8 `module-kit`). So what T06 left as it was is corrected — the comments about the Go module of `spec` and CodeQL (RUN.md, T06);
  - R80 spec with its 2026-11-02 amendments — the carrying over of progress and an attempt without evidence — is about `api` and `learner-model`: the module gives them `retired_skills`, as before (R49 spec). R83 spec with its amendments to R11, R21, R44, R64, R72 and R73 spec — the API contracts on protobuf. The amendments to R36 and R74 spec — `app` gives the pictures to the canvas element by the function `media` from the device part. They do not change the documents of `modules`.
- **What changed here:**

Both templates were built by `new-repo-draft` of their own tag for `modules` of the kind `docs` in the session's folder. The tag and rule 2 of RUN.md changed (R81 spec), and the opening of the log is its own;
