## The first batch

**R07. The first batch of `novatrail/olympiad-math` is 27 tasks of standalone of three skills, `counting-gaps`, `combinatorics-enumeration` and `pigeonhole-basic`, three for each skill and band, and 9 lessons, a lesson per skill and band. The answer of each task is an integer by hand, the module is in English, and two tasks have a picture of a row. The batch's graph is these three skills. Item 2.3 of [architecture.md](architecture.md) is decided in full.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in planning T07 (RUN.md, T07, «Решено автором»). Four questions — the make-up, the language, the pictures and the lessons — were decided by the recommendations, and the rest by the plan that the author approved. The list of the batch is [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6. There is no code: the module's files are written by the content epic, row 3.6 of [COORDINATION.md](../spec/COORDINATION.md).
- **The make-up.** The topics are those that run in all three bands, whose answers are almost all integers and whose page on the site has an example for each level: `counting.gaps`, `combinatorics.enumeration` and `pigeonhole.basic`. For each skill and band three tasks are taken, of difficulty 1, 3 and 5. The rule of choice is section 3.6 ibid.: a task fits whose correct option is an integer, with a unit or without; at each difficulty the first fitting task by ID is taken, without a leak in the lesson and in the explanations; if there is no fitting one, the nearest difficulty is taken. The difficulty shifted for one task — `counting-gaps` 1–2, from 1 to 2.
- **The answer.** Each task has one item: an answer slot `anywhere` and the check `number` with the key as a string. The question of every task of the batch counts objects or a measure, so every check has `units` — the word in the singular and the plural, and for a measure also the abbreviation: without them "14 metres" by hand would not be in the number's notation ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15). An answer with a unit, like "50 m" of `gaps-56-d1-2`, is written the same way: the key is a number, and the unit is in `units` (R05).
  - All the wrong options of the 27 tasks are numbers, and all of them become trap values, without a unit (R05). There are no traps in `traps` from the port.
  - No task of the batch asks "Which of these…", and it has no word answers. Whether answers that are not numbers are written by tapping or by hand the rest of the port decides (R05).
  - Two explanations name the key — those of `enum-12-d1-3` and `pig-56-d5-1` — and they are rewritten.
- **The graph.** The skills `counting-gaps` and `combinatorics-enumeration` stand in tier 1, `pigeonhole-basic` in tier 2, after `combinatorics-enumeration`. This is not a chain, so the module is not sequential (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R58 spec). The tier names are all three names of the format example: a name beyond the last tier names nothing, and the port will add tier 3 without touching them. There are no anchor skills, so there is no `dependencies` either (`manifest.dependencies`). The port adds the other 14 skills.
- **The manifest** — by the format example `olympiad-math`. `age_categories` — `under_13` and `adult` (R76 spec), the explanation language `en`, without `dependencies`. The content epic writes the version.
- **The catalogs:**
  - the traps and techniques — only the entries of the format example that the batch's tasks name: 10 traps and 4 techniques. None of these traps has `skills`, and the example's six traps tied to skills outside the batch would break `traps.known-skills`;
  - the solving steps — all four steps of the example.
- **Every task has `givens`, `unknown`, techniques and the `simpler_case` of its item.** Before the first wrong answer the hint gives "givens and unknown" and a technique (R82 spec), step 1 ticks the chips, and `simpler_case` is the text of rung 3 when there are no tutor's words ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 19). The techniques are by skill: those that standalone's site links with the topic, and for `counting-gaps` also `draw` — the method of its page. The content epic writes the texts.
- **standalone's hint** of band 5–6 is not carried over. All nine hints are questions that break the task into steps, that is, a path to the answer. `givens` do not tell the path, and `simpler_case` is a simpler similar case ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14). The ladder does without them, and there is no request to 0.y: if the first application shows a need, T38 `spec` will gather it (Д60 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`).
- **The lessons** — a lesson per skill and band, `<skill>-grades-<band>`. Each task of the batch stands in exactly one lesson. The order of the segments:
  1. an explanation — the gist of the topic and its rules from the topic page;
  2. a worked example — the page's example of its own level, its steps and answer;
  3. the example's note "Why not…", if it has one;
  4. the band's three tasks in increasing difficulty;
  5. an explanation — the steps of the page's method.

  A lesson's title is the page's title and the band's words, without numbers. There are no pictures in the lessons. The lessons of `pigeonhole-basic` come after the lessons of `combinatorics-enumeration` by the graph (R58 spec). A lesson has no band, so the format puts every lesson of `pigeonhole-basic` after every lesson of `combinatorics-enumeration`, after the lesson of 5–6 too. Which of a skill's three lessons to give a learner, how to read the order of lessons of different bands and in what order the lessons of `counting-gaps` and `combinatorics-enumeration` go is chosen by the engine's rule (R58 spec) — В14 in table 3 of RUN.md.
- **The pictures.** The picture `row` stands at `gaps-12-d2-1` and `gaps-34-d1-1`. This is a PNG in `media/`, drawn by the code of the content epic from standalone's description, with `alt`, width, height and on a light opaque background (R51 spec). These tasks have no solution pictures.
- **The language** is English. Such is the language of standalone's tasks and of the format examples, and it is part of any option of О-2. The same module in Russian would become another module, with its own name ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1).

— *Why:*
- **Three skills of nine tasks each**: in each skill the engine has something to choose from in each band, and the graph gives an order of lessons (`pigeonhole-basic` after `combinatorics-enumeration`) and a choice between skills without a link. The answers of these topics are numbers, except three of `counting-gaps` 5–6 and two of `pigeonhole-basic` 3–4.
- **Only numbers by hand**: R76 spec requires handwriting from the first day, and a number is what the measurement of `tutor` read on a sheet with an answer slot `anywhere`. There is neither a tapped choice nor a choice by hand in the batch, and the question about them does not arise.
- **English**: there is no translation, the leaks are not checked anew, and О-2 is not prejudged.
- **A picture of a row at two tasks**: the first application will pass the path `media/` → the device part → `app` on a real module. The epic's code needs one kind of picture out of 13.
- **A lesson per skill and band**: the three topics have on the site's page exactly one example per level, and a learner of band 1–2 does not get to the tasks of 5–6 in a lesson. The format does not distinguish the order of lessons of different bands, and the engine's rule reads it — В14.
- **A graph of three skills**: a skill without tasks and lessons gives the engine nothing, and the graph is closed under prerequisites ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 12).
- **`givens`, `unknown`, techniques and `simpler_case`**: without them the request "Hint" before the first wrong answer gives nothing (R82 spec).

— *Rejected:*
- the chain `counting-gaps` → `time-calendar`, 30 tasks, like the module of the format examples `with-lessons`: the calendar has 13 number answers of 25 in band 1–2, and the rest are days of the week and months;
- eight topics, a task per topic and band: within a skill the engine has nothing to choose from, and the answers of `logic-ordering` and `time-clocks` are words and times, that is, a choice;
- Russian: a translation of the tasks, explanations and solutions, a new check of leaks, and О-2 decided ahead of `spec`;
- a batch without pictures: the first application would show not one picture of a real module; pictures of every kind the chosen tasks have: several kinds of drawing;
- a lesson per skill with the example of 1–2, and the tasks of 3–4 and 5–6 outside the lessons; a lesson per skill with all the bands: a learner of 1–2 would reach the tasks of 5–6 in the lesson;
- all 17 skills in the graph: 14 skills without tasks;
- a field for the hint in 0.y now: the ladder does not need it, and standalone's hints are a path to the answer, which the format does not hold.

— *Consequence:*
- [architecture.md](architecture.md): subsection 2.3 is decided in full, the batch's list is section 3.6, and the sources are section 4;
- RUN.md: T07 is done; two rows go «в реестр spec» into table 3 — В13, the module's language, to О-2 and product 10 spec, and В14, lessons by bands, to architecture 3.8 spec;
- the module's files, the rewritten explanations, `givens`, `unknown` and `simpler_case`, the code of the picture of a row and the run of the solvers are written by the content epic.

— RUN.md, T07; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2, 2.3, 3, 3.5, 3.6 and 4; R05.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10 (T51):* **the hints of nine tasks of the first batch are leading questions `leading_question`.** The point "standalone's hint of band 5–6 is not carried over" rested on this: a hint is a path to the answer, and the format had nowhere to put it. Format 0.4 gave such a path a closed field, which `api` gives on "Hint" before the first wrong answer (R108 spec; the 2026-11-08 amendment to R82 spec), and the converter carries the hint over word for word (the T51 amendment to R12). The texts of the nine — `enum-56-d1-1`, `enum-56-d3-2`, `enum-56-d5-2`, `gaps-56-d1-2`, `gaps-56-d3-1`, `gaps-56-d5-1`, `pig-56-d1-1`, `pig-56-d3-2` and `pig-56-d5-1` — are the same at 89986bc as at 42b4408. Rejected: leaving the first batch without questions while the other 302 get them — the nine tasks of band 5–6 would be poorer than their neighbours; rewriting the nine — they have no leak, and a question that breaks the task down is exactly what the field is for. — RUN.md, T51; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6.

## The outcome of the detailing

**R08. The detailing of `modules` is finished: items 2.1–2.3 of [architecture.md](architecture.md) are decided (R03–R05, R07). Section 2 names only what is deliberately deferred, and section 3.7 what to build. The next epics are the first content (E2), the rest of the port (E3) and the eval sets (E4). T04 goes at `spec` v0.6.5, and the move to v0.6.6, which answered В2–В9, is put off — Д4 in table 4 of RUN.md.** The author's answer 2026-11-02 in the course of T04 (RUN.md, T04, «Решено автором»). The rest is by the task itself. There is no code.
- **Section 2.** Subsections 2.1–2.3 stand under their former numbers: this log and the tasks of RUN.md refer to them. Of each, what remains is the gist, the decision and the task, and "What is known" and "Options" are moved word for word to the end of sections 3.3, 3.4 and 3.5, as in `tutor`. The section's opening names what is deferred and who will decide it, and how a new item enters the section.
- **Section 3.7** is the table "what to build": the part, its section, the epic and what it waits for. The first application waits from `modules` only for epic E2 (R76 spec).
- **The check against the card** (architecture 3.10 spec, at v0.6.5):
  - the four items of "To decide when detailed" are decided by R03 and R04. Two of them, which the card has named since v0.6.5, — the name of the package's file and where CI takes the earlier versions from, — are carried by В12. On `main` of `spec` — v0.6.6 — the card already names the package's archive, its make-up and its address, and writes that all four items are decided: this is the answer to В4. So no row is needed for the layout and the samples, and В12 is closed by the move — Д4;
  - the gate of `sim` "if the graph has changed" — В5. `spec` recorded it: what the gate includes is decided by T03 of `sim`. The change of CI for the gate is Д5 in table 4 of RUN.md;
  - the card does not name the port of standalone, and at v0.6.6 it is the card's only remaining item. R05 and R07 decided it: the card is changed for it by the outcome of the detailing (architecture 3 spec) — a new row В15 of table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec». Into the same row go the authors' samples in the eval set: the card names only the synthetic samples and the volunteers' samples, and the authors' samples are part of the eval set (concepts 3 spec; R04);
  - the language of the module of olympiad maths is not the card but product 10 spec: В13;
  - [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1 and 3 repeat or unfold the rest of what the card says about `modules`.
- **The rows «в реестр spec»:**
  - `spec` recorded В2–В9 on 2026-11-02, all by the recommendations, and В3, В5 and В8 with refinements (`Д80` in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec` on `main`, in v0.6.6). For В3 a comparison with the newest published version is enough, not one with each published version, which the 2026-11-02 amendment to R03 requires. They are closed by the move to v0.6.6 by the list of `Д80` — Д4;
  - В10–В14 stand on `main` of this repository, and the recipe `inbox` of `spec` v0.6.5 finds them. They are not yet in table 5 of the RUN.md of `spec` on `main`;
  - В15 is new.
- **The next epics**, in the order of table 2 of RUN.md:
  - **E2 — the first content**, row 3.6 of [COORDINATION.md](../spec/COORDINATION.md). In it:
    - the first batch by R07 and small sets of school maths for grade 3 and of essays (R41 spec; T38 of `spec`);
    - CI with the binary of `module-kit` with a checked signature (R77 spec), without the gate of `sim` until its first tag (R76 spec);
    - the first release — the packages and the index (R62, R63 spec).

    The epic's first task is the move to v0.6.6, with the closing of В2–В9 and В12. The epic waits for the first release of `module-kit` with code, row 3.3, and the epic's own first release is Д3;
  - **E3 — the rest of the port**, row 3.7: 678 tasks, templates and solvers, 14 skills and the pictures of the other kinds, by R05. It waits for E2;

— *Why:*

## The container and CI

**R10. The CLI of `module-kit` stands in the `toolchain` stage of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` as the binary of the release `v0.2.3`, by its sha256 sum on amd64 and arm64. This is how the container of `modules` differs from the container of `spec`. CI checks the tree `modules/` and each module with the commands `check` and `build`, and the binary's signature with the recipe `release-verify` on every run. The recipes are written in bash in the root `justfile`, and their tests in `node --test`.** The author's answers 2026-11-06 in the planning of T09 (RUN.md, T09, «Решено автором»), all five by the recommendations, and the rest by the plan that the author approved.
- **The binary.** `ARG MODULE_KIT_VERSION`, `MODULE_KIT_SHA256_AMD64` and `MODULE_KIT_SHA256_ARM64` stand in the `toolchain` stage. The install goes by the same `fetch` as buf's: the linux binary lies in the release as it is, without an archive. The sum is checked before the install (R34 spec). Then the first line of `module-kit --version` must be `module-kit <tag>`, otherwise the build stops. The sums are taken from the release's `SHA256SUMS`, which passed `release-verify` before they were written down: "signed by the shared release.yml of .github v0.1.15". Dependabot sees neither the tag nor the two sums, and they are raised by hand, together. The Dockerfile is not in the release's `paths` (R03), so raising the CLI gives no release.
- **The recipes** (`justfile`):
  - `modules-check` checks the tree by R03: in `modules/` only folders, nothing starting with `.` and not one link. Before the first module it checks the first line of `module-kit --version` against `MODULE_KIT_VERSION`: a container not rebuilt after the tag was raised would otherwise check the modules with another CLI than CI and the release. Each folder it gives to `module-kit check <module>`, then to `module-kit build <module> <folder>` into a temporary folder. The ID from the package's `inventory.json` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 25) must be `novatrail/<folder>`. If one module is refused, the check of the others goes on, and the recipe fails at the end. Without `modules/` the recipe prints `modules/: no modules yet` and exits with code 0;
  - `module-kit-verify` takes the tag from the `ARG` and downloads the release's `SHA256SUMS` and its bundle by the public address, without a token. The signature is checked by `just -f spec/justfile release-verify module-kit <tag> <file>` (R77 spec), and `_module-kit-sums` checks the lines of both linux binaries against the sums of the `ARG`;
  - the form of the call of `check` and `build` is held by architecture 3.6 module-kit at v0.2.3. The recipes refer to it, and a test holds the call with a stub `module-kit` that records its arguments (R102 spec).
- **The tests** — `test/*.test.mjs`, `just test`: `node --test`, without npm packages and without a lock file.
  - The tests run the tree and the modules with the real `module-kit` of the `toolchain` stage: the format examples `spec/format/examples/module/valid/` and the vector `module.license/no-license` — in temporary repositories, with the ID `novatrail/<folder>`.
  - The form of the call and the order are held by the stub, and the check of the sums by a made-up `SHA256SUMS` and Dockerfile, without the network.
  - `release-verify` itself is not tested here: the recipe of `spec` has its own tests, and the CI job runs it for real.
- **CI** (`ci.yml`) — two jobs of its own beside the shared ones. They build the `toolchain` stage and run the recipes in it with the script `spec/tools/toolchain/toolchain.sh` from the submodule, at the tag of `spec`:
  - "module-kit signature" — `just module-kit-verify` on every pull request and push to `main`;
  - "Modules" — `needs` the first; `just test`, then `just modules-check`.

  The workflow has no filter `paths` (R03). CodeQL has the language `javascript-typescript`, for the test files. Codecov is not connected: Node measures coverage only of JavaScript, and the code under check is bash, and the check `codecov/patch` has nothing to measure (R48 spec).
- **The binary's first run** is `module-kit --version` at the image build, before the signature job. The signature is held by a chain of digests:
  - `SHA256SUMS` was checked by `release-verify` before its sums stood in the `ARG`;
  - the build checks the binary against the sum before running it;
  - in CI "Modules" runs `check` and `build` only when "module-kit signature" has passed.

  From then on the sum holds the binary (R77 spec; RULES.md, Hard rules).

— *Why:*
- **bash and `node --test`**: this is how `spec` writes and tests its recipes, and the recipes here are a thin wrapper over the CLI and `release-verify`. The Node tests need not one package, which means there is neither a lock file nor a check of its licenses. If code that Codecov measures comes with the converter of T11, then Codecov and `codecov/patch` will come too.
- **`toolchain.sh` from `spec`**: `spec` wrote it for the workflows of other repositories. `.github` v0.1.15 has no shared workflow that runs whichever of the caller's recipes the caller chooses: `documents.yml`, `go.yml` and `typescript.yml` each run their own recipes. The script comes with the tag of `spec` and already holds how to build the stage and run a command in it.
- **The signature on every run**: one check for all, without a rule "have the `ARG` changed" and its tests, and without a check that reports now green, now skipped. The price is the build of the image of the `toolchain` stage in the signature job and the network of Sigstore and GitHub on every run, and "Modules" starts only when the signature job has ended. The check itself takes seconds, and the image build minutes, so a CI run is longer by one build. If Sigstore is unavailable, the merge waits.
- **The ID from `inventory.json`**: `module-kit` writes it as canonical text, and the `jq` of the base image, pinned by digest, reads it without a YAML reader.
- **Without `modules/` — green**: git keeps no empty folders, so this is a tree without modules, and until T12, T14 or T15 it is correct.

— *Rejected:*
- tests in bash: neither CodeQL nor SonarCloud would get a new language, but the tests are harder to read and maintain;
- recipes and tests in Go in `tools/`, with the shared `go.yml` and Codecov: heavier than what the recipes need now. The language of the converter is decided by T11;
- `docker build` and `docker run` right in the steps of `ci.yml`, as in `documents.yml`: this is a copy of what `toolchain.sh` holds;
- the signature only on a pull request that changes the `ARG` of `module-kit`, as R03 said: the rule "changed", with the base of the pull request and the first parent on a push, would have to be written and tested;
- a red job until the first module: it would hold the merge of every pull request until T12;
- one job "Modules" with the steps "signature → tests → modules": the image would be built once, but the task names two jobs, and the signature check is seen as a separate check of the ruleset;
- checking the signature of each binary instead of `SHA256SUMS`: two files of 7 MB on every run instead of one of 1.3 KB. The sum of each binary is a line of the checked `SHA256SUMS`, so the chain is the same;
- the ID from `manifest.yaml`: a reader of YAML 1.2.2 with the format's restrictions would be needed, and `module-kit` has already read it and written the ID into the inventory.

— *Consequence:*
- an amendment to R03 — the signature on every run;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.2, 3.7 and 4;
- CLAUDE.md, Environment — the difference of the container;
- README.md — the status. There is no language badge: repositories 2.3 spec sets it by the Go version from `go.mod` or the TypeScript version from `package.json`, and here there is neither. The CI recipes and their tests are not code of the platform, like the check tools of `spec`, which has no language badge either;
- the checks "module-kit signature", "Modules" and "CodeQL / analyze (javascript-typescript)" stand in the ruleset of `main`, 25506537, since 2026-11-06, after their first green run on pull request #19 (rule 4 of RUN.md; RUN.md, T09).

— RUN.md, T09; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `justfile`; `test/`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `codeql.yml`; R03.

**R11. CI compares each module with the published versions with the command `module-kit diff` — in the recipe `modules-check`, on the package that `build` built. The published versions are the index of the newest release of `modules` — the greatest tag `vX.Y.Z` from `git ls-remote` that has an index, — after `release-verify`, and the packages of all the versions of the modules of `modules/` by the index's entries. Without a release the recipe says `--first` explicitly. The recipe's code is the greatest of the codes of what failed, so the code of `diff` is the job's code.** T10 (RUN.md), 2026-11-06: a task without the planning mode, and the choice below is the executor's, by R03 with its amendments and architecture 3.5 module-kit.
- **The recipes** (`justfile`):
  - `modules-check` — `_published` into a temporary folder, then `_modules-check` over it. Without modules — there is no `modules/` or it is empty — it prints `modules/: no modules yet` and does not go to the network. A failure of `_published` gives code 2: the modules were not compared, not rejected;
  - `_published <folder>`:
    - the tags — `git ls-remote --tags --refs https://github.com/altnovatrail/modules 'refs/tags/v*'`, without a token and without the caller's git settings. `_release-tags` keeps the tags `vX.Y.Z` and orders them from the newest by the numbers of their parts, and other tags are not releases of `modules`;
    - the newest release is the first of them whose `module-index.json` is at the public address. A tag whose index answers 404 is a release that did not come out: the log names it, and the tag below is taken. Such a tag can be neither moved nor deleted — the ruleset `v-tags`, — and without this rule it would hold CI of every pull request. Any other failure fails the recipe;
    - there is no release — the file `first`, and nothing more;
    - there is a release — the index's bundle by the same address, then `just -f spec/justfile release-verify modules <tag> …` (R77 spec). For each folder of `modules/` — the entries `novatrail/<folder>`, withdrawn ones too, by `_entries`: a line per version — the version, the release and the digest, — and a release not of the form `vX.Y.Z` and a digest not `sha256:` with 64 digits are rejected, and no addresses are built from them. Each archive is downloaded by the address from the entry ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3), and `_unpack` puts the package into `packages/sha256-<hex>/`. An archive that is not at the address fails the recipe. All the files are downloaded by `_fetch` — https only, with retries, like the `fetch` of the Dockerfile; code 1 — the address answered 404, 2 — any other failure. `module-kit-verify` downloads with it too;
  - `_unpack <archive> <folder>` takes only `inventory.json`, `server.json` and `device.json`, each once, as a regular file, with `./` before the name or without. An archive with a link — symbolic or hard, — a special file, an absolute path or a path through `..` is rejected whole, before unpacking. The files themselves are held by digests: `diff` finds the package by the digest of `inventory.json` from the entry and checks `server.json` and `device.json` by the inventory;
  - `_modules-check <folder>` requires in the folder exactly one of the two — `module-index.json` or `first`. After `check`, `build` and the ID check it calls `module-kit diff --index <index> --published <package>… <package from build>` with the packages of this module's entries, and with `first` — `module-kit diff --first <package from build>`.
- **The recipe's code** is the greatest of the codes of what failed: 1 — the tree, R03 and a refusal by the rules of the format or of versions, 2 — `module-kit` could not do its work, `diff` without the package of a version from the index among them. The refusal of one module still does not stop the check of the others (R10).
- **The tests** — `test/published.test.mjs`, without the network:
  - `_release-tags` — on lines of `git ls-remote`; `_entries` — versions with a withdrawn one, a module that the index does not name, entries without an address and an index without the object `modules`;
  - `modules-check` without `modules/` — with `git` and `curl` that fail if they are called;
  - `_unpack` — on archives that the tests build with `tar`: a regular package, names with `./`, a symbolic and a hard link, paths outside the folder, a missing file, a file twice, an empty archive, not an archive, the folder already exists;
  - `_modules-check` — on releases that the tests build with the real `module-kit` — `build` and `index --first`: the same version with the same digest, the same version with another — `module-version.above`, a new version, no package of a version from the index — code 2, a module that the index does not name, `--first`, none or both of `module-index.json` and `first`. The form of the call and the greatest code — `diff` with codes 2 and 1 for different modules — are held by the stub.

  The tests do not touch the signature and the network: `release-verify` is held by the tests of `spec`, and `git ls-remote`, `_fetch` and `_published` run in CI for real. By hand on 2026-11-06: `_published` without a release writes `first`, `_fetch` gives code 0 on the `SHA256SUMS` of `module-kit` v0.2.3 and 1 on a file that is not in the release. Until the first release CI takes the path without a release, and the path with an index for the first time on the first pull request after the first release (RUN.md, T10, «Риски»; T16).

— *Why:*
- **The greatest tag from `git ls-remote`**: the tags `v*` are set only by a release, and the ruleset `v-tags` holds them (R31 spec; Д3 in table 4 of RUN.md). This is how `release-verify` reads the releases of `.github` too: through git, without the REST API and without a token (R77 spec). The count is by numbers, not by text: `v0.10.0` is newer than `v0.9.9`.
- **The file `first`**: "there is no release" and "the index did not download" must differ, and `diff` requires one of the two to be said explicitly (architecture 3.5 module-kit). A network failure fails the recipe and does not turn into `--first`. Only a tag whose index answers 404 can be skipped, and the log names it: the versions of such a release are not published, and the comparison with the release before it is what is needed.
- **Without modules — without the network**: a tree without modules has nothing to compare, and a failure of GitHub or Sigstore must not hold a pull request that does not touch modules.
- **Three files from the archive**: `diff` reads the package without `device/`, and a link or a path outside the folder in an archive from the network is a reason to unpack nothing. Nothing holds the archive's bytes ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3), and the digests are checked by `diff`.
- **The package from `build`, not the module's folder**: `diff` over the module's folder would check it a second time, with the solvers (architecture 3.5 module-kit).
- **The greatest code**: T10 requires the code of `diff` to be the job's code, and the recipe has many calls; 2 distinguishes "could not compare" from "refusal".

— *Rejected:*
- the newest release by `releases/latest`: "latest" is a mark of GitHub that can be set by hand, not the greatest tag;
- the REST API for the list of releases: a token or its rate limit, and T10 requires doing without a token;
- downloading the archives of all the modules of the index: `diff` needs only the versions of the modules that are being checked;
- `file://` and a substitution of addresses, so that the tests go through `_published` without the network: the path `curl --proto =https` would have to be weakened for the sake of the tests;
- accepting only names without `./`: how the archive is built is decided by T16, and `diff` reads the same three files;
- failing on a tag without an index, as on any failure: the tag cannot be deleted, and CI would stand until a manual intervention in `.github` or the settings;
- taking the newest tag without asking for the index and failing without it — the argument above.

— *Consequence:* [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.7 and 4; `ci.yml` — the comment of the job "Modules"; CLAUDE.md, Environment. — RUN.md, T10; `justfile`; `test/`; R03; R10.

## The port of standalone's tasks

**R12. standalone's tasks are carried over into the module by the converter `tools/port` — Go code of this repository, the module `github.com/altnovatrail/modules` with `go.mod` at the root. The key and the trap values are proven by standalone's own test bench in its temporary clone. What a person writes lies in the overlay files `tools/port/overlay/<id>.yaml`, and the converter writes the task file whole and the same on every run. It draws the picture `row` as PNG. The code is checked by the shared `go.yml` of `.github`, and the coverage goes to Codecov.** The author's answers 2026-11-06 in the planning of T11 (RUN.md, T11, «Решено автором»), all five by the recommendations, and the rest by the plan that the author approved.
- **The command** — `go run ./tools/port --standalone <clone> --batch <batch file> --overlay <overlay folder> --module <module folder> [--prove] [--check] [--go <command>] [--git <command>]`: the command `go` of the test bench and the command `git` of the clone's check come by flags, by default from `PATH`, and the tests substitute them. The recipe `just port <module> [--batch <file>] [--overlay <folder>] [--check]` clones standalone at the batch's commit into a temporary folder (RULES.md, Citations) and calls the command with `--prove`. Codes: 0 — written or matched, 1 — `--check` found a mismatch, 2 — could not.
- **Provenance.** The clone must stand at the batch's commit, without changes of its own: `git rev-parse HEAD` and an empty `git status --porcelain`, git without the caller's settings. Otherwise the converter writes nothing: the tasks, their header and the test bench are all from one commit.
- **The batch** — `tools/port/batches/<name>.yaml`: the full SHA of standalone's commit, the tasks' canvas, for each skill its techniques and anchor skills, for each task its `units`. The first is `first.yaml`, the 27 tasks of R07 with the units and techniques of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6. The new batches of E3 are new files.
- **The task** — by the table "what becomes what" of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5:
  - the topic is `skills.primary`, the band `grades-<a>-<b>`, the difficulty the same;
  - `question` is the first part of `statement`, the picture the second; `solution` and `solution_total` are the parts of `solution`;
  - a correct option in the format's number notation — an integer or a decimal without leading zeros, with a unit from the batch's `units` after a space or without one, — is an answer slot `anywhere` with the check `number`, and a simple fraction `a/b` is one with `fraction` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15). The key is no longer than 16 characters;
  - the question "Which of these…" is an answer slot `choice` by tapping, with five options and the key — the text of the correct one. Another answer — a word, a time, a date — the converter rejects: how to give it is decided by E3 (section 3.5 there);
  - a wrong option that the check reads is a trap value, with standalone's explanation or a replacement from the overlay file: for `number` this includes a simple fraction, and for `fraction` only a simple fraction. An option that the check does not read — a word, a time, a number with a foreign unit, — gives its trap into the task's `traps`, and the report names it: its explanation is not carried over. A value is no longer than 16 characters, and none equals the key or another value in the sense of the check;
  - `hint` is not carried over, and the report names each such task; fields of a kind other than the converter knows are a refusal.
- **The overlay file** — `givens`, `unknown`, `simpler_case`, `techniques` in place of the skill's techniques, extra `traps`, `leak_phrases`, `picture_alt` and `solution_picture_alt`, `trap_texts` by trap value. A refusal, so that an edit is not lost silently: there is no overlay folder, it has an unknown field or a file of a task that the batch does not name, a replacement of the text of a value that the task does not have, an `alt` of a picture that does not exist. A picture without `alt` is a refusal too.
- **The task file** is written by a small YAML emitter of its own under the format's restrictions ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 4): keys without quotes, strings in double quotes as JSON writes them, without `\/`, integers. The header comment names standalone's commit, the topic file and the overlay file. The comment does not get into the package.
- **The converter's files** of a task are `tasks/<id>.yaml` and its two pictures. Such a file of a batch's task that the run no longer writes is stale: `--check` names it, and the run removes it. The tasks of other batches the converter does not touch.
- **The check against the solver** — `--prove`: `go -C <clone> test ./content -run '^TestEveryReferenceTaskProvesItsOwnAnswer$/^(<ID>|…)$' -count=1 -json`. The test bench runs the task's solver in standalone's sandbox, twice with the letters swapped, requires exactly one letter and checks it against `correct_answer`. The converter requires `pass` of the subtest of each task of the batch and otherwise writes nothing, and a refusal carries the output of the subtest that failed and the build output of `go test -json`. The key is the number of the correct option, so the test bench proves the key and that no trap value equals it.
- **The picture `row`** — `media/pictures/<id>.png`, and the solution picture `<id>-solution.png`. standalone's description is read strictly: 2–12 objects, each with the mark `dot`, `ring` or `square`, labels above and below, or a gap — not the first, not the last and not next to another. The row has `line`, `gaps`, `span` and `copies`. The drawing is on a white opaque background, with twice as many pixels as CSS px, with the anti-aliasing of `golang.org/x/image/vector` and labels in the font Go Regular. The PNG has only IHDR, IDAT and IEND, and the bytes are the same on every run: they go into the package digest. Other kinds of pictures — E3.
- **Dependencies** — `golang.org/x/image` v0.47.0, BSD-3-Clause, as in `module-kit`, with its `golang.org/x/text` and `golang.org/x/sys`, BSD-3-Clause, and `go.yaml.in/yaml/v4` v4.0.0, Apache-2.0 with MIT files from libyaml, only for reading the batch and the overlay files. All are compatible with Apache-2.0 (R12 spec), and `ci-licenses` lets them through.
- **CI.** The job "Go" calls the shared `go.yml` v0.1.15. The checks — "Go / lint", "Go / test", "Go / vuln" and "Go / licenses" — run by the recipes `ci-lint`, `ci-test`, `ci-vuln` and `ci-licenses` of the `justfile`, after the pattern of `spec`: govulncheck v1.9.0 and go-licenses v2.0.1 through `go run`. The profile `coverage/go.out` goes to Codecov by OIDC. `.github/codecov.yml` is the text of the template of architecture 3.3 .github: `codecov/patch` ≥ 90%. CodeQL has the language `go`, Dependabot `gomod`. Go is not in the release's `paths`.
- **Tests.** The Go tests are `tools/port/*_test.go`, on tasks in standalone's form with texts of their own: they do not read `spec/`, which `go.yml` does not take. They are the cases of the table — an integer, a number with a unit, a fraction, "Which of these…", a non-numeric wrong option, — the refusals, the overlay file, the pictures, the test bench with a substituted `go` and the command itself; the coverage is 97%. The Node test `test/port.test.mjs` in the job "Modules" writes tasks with a picture into a module from the format examples `olympiad-math`, and `module-kit check` accepts it without warnings.

— *Why:*
- **Go in the repository**: Go already stands in the `toolchain` stage, the picture is drawn by its standard library with `x/image`, and E3 will carry over 678 more tasks with the same code. The shared `go.yml` gives the checks and the coverage without a CI of its own.
- **standalone's test bench**: the solvers call the helpers of its sandbox — for the batch `match`, `product`, `combinations` and `days_in_month`, for E3 more of them. A go.starlark.net of our own would have to be extended with these helpers, and it could diverge from what standalone has already proven. standalone is frozen, and its test bench at 89986bc will not change.
- **Overlay files**: a converter corrected after the batch writes its tasks anew, and what was written by hand is not lost; `--check` catches a task file edited by hand.
- **An emitter of its own**: under the restrictions of the format's section 4 a small subset of YAML is written, and its bytes do not depend on the version of someone else's library.

— *Rejected:*
- Node in `tools/port/`: a PNG with labels needs npm packages, a lock file and the check of its licenses;
- code outside the repository: E3 would write it anew, and it would have no tests in CI;
- a go.starlark.net of our own with the format's dialect (R88 spec) — the argument above;
- "the first run, then by hand": a fix of the converter would not reach the tasks already written;
- only the standard library for PNG: a font of digits of our own would have to be drawn and maintained;
- two tasks instead of one, T11 and T18 for the picture: the author decided on one.

— *Consequence:*
- T12 writes the overlay files of 27 tasks — `givens`, `unknown`, `simpler_case` and the rewritten explanations of `enum-12-d1-3` and `pig-56-d5-1` — and runs `just port modules/olympiad-math`. The `alt` of the two pictures `row` lies in the overlay files already now;
- the checks "Go / lint", "Go / test", "Go / vuln", "Go / licenses" and "CodeQL / analyze (go)", and with the first upload also `codecov/patch`, go into the ruleset of `main` by rule 4 of RUN.md;
- there is no language badge in the README: the code is a tool of the port, like the check tools of `spec`, not code of the platform (repositories 2.3 spec). The coverage badge comes with the first upload to Codecov on `main`;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.5, 3.7 and 4; CLAUDE.md, Environment.

— RUN.md, T11; `go.mod`; `tools/port/`; `test/port.test.mjs`; `justfile`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `codeql.yml`; `.github/dependabot.yml`; `.github/codecov.yml`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T18):* **the converter reads standalone at 42b4408 too and writes an answer of any kind (R20).**
- **Refusals.** An answer that is not a number it no longer rejects. A number with words without the batch's `units` is no longer the refusal "needs the units" but a non-number, and the report names it. New refusals: a topic not from `content/catalogs/topics.json`, a task of a topic that is solved from the picture without a picture, an unknown field of the topic catalog, an explanation and a text of the overlay file that name the key of a choice, an `answer_form` that names the key of any answer, and options of a choice that are equal in the normal form.
- **Fields.** The task's `idea` is read and not carried over. The overlay file has a new field, `answer_form`.
- **Flags and the recipe.** A new mode `--survey` with `--commit` and `--except`, and the recipe `just port-survey`. Code 1 of the command now also means a refusal to a task in the run.
- **Dependencies.** `golang.org/x/text` v0.43.0 is now a direct dependency, for the sake of `unicode/norm`.
- **The test bench.** The words "standalone is frozen, and its test bench at 89986bc will not change" are still true for 89986bc (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R05). The test bench of 42b4408 proves the tasks with the same test.

— RUN.md, T18; `tools/port/`; `justfile`; `go.mod`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T19):* **the converter draws five more kinds of standalone's pictures: `clock`, `table`, `bars`, `number_line` and `calendar`.**
- **The structure.** What is shared is in `tools/port/picture.go`: a kind of picture is read strictly, by its own `$defs` in `content/schemas/task.json` of standalone at 42b4408, and checked against what standalone's service checks (`internal/domain/picture/`) and the schema cannot name. The names of members are checked exactly: `encoding/json` by itself would let through `TIME` in place of `time`, and a member named twice. Each kind is a file of its own, with the check, the size and the drawing. The drawing surface, the font Go Regular of any size and the writing of PNG are shared, and `row` moved onto them without changing a byte of its pictures: `just port modules/olympiad-math --check` gives the same 27 tasks.
- **What is checked beyond the schema:** for a table, each row is as long as the header or the first row; for a bar, parts or segments, not both, no more shaded than there are parts, and a bracket lies within the bar; for a line, `to` is greater than `from`, the step reaches `to` in whole steps, 2–16 ticks, a mark is on a tick, one per tick; for a calendar, the day of a mark is written without a leading zero and is in the month.
- **The drawing** follows the picture of standalone's card (`web/src/design/picture/`), without its layout of labels:
  - the clock — a dial of 200 × 200 CSS px with the numbers 1–12 and minute ticks; the hour hand is shifted by the minutes, a time after 12:59 is as on a twelve-hour dial; the label of a hand is beside it, on the side where there is no other hand, and for hands that lie together, on opposite sides;
  - the table — the header on a grey background, a column as wide as its longest cell;
  - the bars — at one scale, a unit no longer than 28 CSS px, and the longest bar no longer than 400. A bar's length is `length`, otherwise the sum of the segments or the number of parts. Shaded parts are grey. Brackets are under the bar, in rows without overlaps. Notes are under all the bars;
  - the number line — ticks with numbers under them, negative ones with a minus sign, and an arrow to the right; a mark is a dot on a tick, and its label is above it;
  - the calendar — the names of the days in English, from Monday or Sunday; a day with a mark is in a frame, and the label is under it.
- **PNG.** A large picture Go writes in several IDAT chunks. This is the same chunk, so the file still has only IHDR, IDAT and IEND.
- **The run of 2026-11-08.** `just port-survey` at 42b4408 writes 641 tasks of 726. 85 wait for their pictures — the kinds of T20 and T21. `module-kit check` warns about not one picture, and the violations of `task.no-leaks` are now 140: new tasks are written.

— RUN.md, T19; `tools/port/`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T20):* **the converter draws six more kinds: `containers`, `grid`, `piles`, `venn`, `balance` and `ring`**, with the same structure as in T19.
- **What is checked beyond the schema:** for a container, no more is poured than its capacity; for a grid, the names of rows and columns do not repeat, and those joined into a cell's name do not give two cells with one name, a shaded cell and a mark are cells of the grid, and a shaded one only once; for piles, a gap is not the first, not the last and not next to another, and holds nothing but itself, and `shown` is only on a pile with a count, from 2 to one less than the count.
- **The drawing** follows the picture of standalone's card:
  - containers — open at the top, standing on one floor, as high as their capacity at one scale, with a tick on the wall for each unit. Water is grey. How much is poured — in the water, and where it is shallow, above the water or above the container. The capacity is under the container, the mark above them all;
  - the grid — square cells, the names of rows on the left, of columns at the top, shaded cells grey, a mark in its cell;
  - piles — counters as circles or squares, dark or light, with a space after each group. A line breaks between groups. A cut pile is half the counters, an ellipsis and the other half; a pile without a count is a counter, an ellipsis, a counter; an empty one is a dashed place. The mark is before the pile, the value after it. Piles go one under another or, with `across`, side by side, in a shared box with `box` and in a box of their own with `boxed`; a gap is three dots. The number of counters is not written: the counters show it;
  - Euler circles — two circles in a frame. Above each, its mark and how many are in it, "F: 15"; how many are in both — in the intersection; in neither — in the lower corner of the frame; the total — on the upper side of the frame;
  - the balance — two pans on posts above the beam, weights with marks in rows of two from the pan upward;
  - the ring — places as circles numbered from 1 at the top clockwise, and arrows on the ring between them. The start place is grey, and its mark is in the middle of the ring, with an arrow to it.
- **The drawing surface** got `outline` — the contour of a circle without fill: so the Euler circles do not erase each other.
- **The check of member names from T19** let through only lowercase names, and the keys of `marks` of a grid are names of cells, "A1". The keys of `marks` of a grid and of a calendar — and only those, at the top level of the picture, — are now checked only for repeats: they are data, not names of the schema. `marks` of another kind is checked by name, like everything else.
- **The run of 2026-11-08.** `just port-survey` at 42b4408 writes 682 tasks of 726, and the test bench proves all of them. 44 wait for their pictures — the kinds of T21. There are no refusals, `module-kit check` warns about not one picture, and the violations of `task.no-leaks` are 157.

— RUN.md, T20; `tools/port/`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T22):* **each batch of E3 has a folder of overlay files of its own, `tools/port/overlay/<batch>/<id>.yaml`.** The converter refuses an overlay file of a task that the batch does not name, and in one folder the overlay files of a new batch would break the first one. The overlay files of the first batch moved to `tools/port/overlay/first/`, and the converter still refuses any folder inside the overlay folder. The recipe `just port` takes the overlay folder by the batch's name, so a batch of E3 is run with one command: `just port modules/olympiad-math --batch tools/port/batches/<batch>.yaml`. The header of a task file names the overlay file in the batch's folder; in the tasks of the first batch only the header changed, and the comment does not get into the package. — RUN.md, T22; `tools/port/`; `justfile`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T26):* **`givens` and `unknown` may name a key that the question names — beside another option or in the words of the question itself.** They retell the statement, and a word written by hand always stands in the question, by R20: "The beads go red, blue, red, blue" and "The first bead is red." are a retelling, not an answer. `task.no-leaks` does not look for a key that stands in the statement ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20), so such a retelling does not give it away. But the `givens` "Ben runs slowest.", which are not in the question and have no other option, are an answer, and the converter refuses them. It also refuses when `simpler_case` names the key, and when `givens` or `unknown` name it in a task whose question does not name it. The converter does not count the picture here, unlike for the `alt` (R21): this is stricter than the format's check. Rejected: letting the key into any `givens` of a task whose question names it — `/code-review` of T26 showed that `givens` that simply say the answer pass that way. — RUN.md, T26; `tools/port/convert.go`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T29):* **"in the words of the question itself" means in a fragment of the question, even one without a full stop at the end, that has, besides the key, at least three more words.** In the question of `cal-56-d1-2`, "13 February 2026 is a Friday, and …", the retelling is followed by a comma, while the `givens` have a full stop, and the converter did not find the retelling in the question. Now it drops the signs of a sentence's end — `.`, `!`, `?`, `;`, `:` — and looks for the rest. But then the question would also yield the scrap "a Friday.", which only names the key, so three more words are needed besides the words of the key. Rejected: dropping only the full stop — `/code-review` of T29 showed that "Friday." passes that way; comparing with whole parts of the question between punctuation marks — "and its 1st day is a Monday" would not match "Its 1st day is a Monday.". — RUN.md, T29; `tools/port/convert.go`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T31):* **`containers` is drawn without ticks on the walls.** standalone's service puts a line on the wall of a container for each unit, and the converter of T20 repeated it. But every task of standalone with this picture is a pouring task, and six of them say outright "The jugs have no marks": with ticks a child would measure 2 litres in one step, and the picture would argue with the statement. The capacity and the amount are still written as numbers. Rejected: drawing the ticks, as standalone does — the picture contradicts the task; drawing them by a flag of the batch — there is not one task with ticks. — RUN.md, T31; `tools/port/containers.go`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T41), by the executor, on the author's instruction:* **the converter draws the pictures of lessons too: the mode `--pictures <folder> --module <module>` takes each file `<name>.json` of the folder — a picture in standalone's schema of pictures — and draws it, as it draws a task's picture, into `media/lessons/<name>.png` of the module, without a clone; extra files of `media/lessons/` it deletes, and with `--check` it writes nothing and names each file that differs.** The data of the module's lesson pictures lie in `tools/port/pictures/<module folder>/`, the recipe is `just lesson-pictures <module> [--check]`, and CI checks `modules/olympiad-math` with the step "The pictures of the lessons" of the job Modules: this mode asks for no clone of standalone, and it takes the Go modules as the port's test `test/port.test.mjs` takes them. The size of a picture in a lesson is half the size of its PNG, as for a task's picture, and the test `test/lesson-pictures.test.mjs` checks it: a lesson is written by hand, and a picture can be redrawn. The checking and the writing of files are shared by the mode and the carrying over of tasks, the functions `filesDiffer` and `placeFiles`. Rejected: drawing the lessons' pictures with a script outside the repository — the PNG in the module could not be checked against the data; drawing them with the code of standalone's site, with Preact components, — that is the build of the site, and the data of the site's drawings are the same `lattice` that the converter already draws. — RUN.md, T41; the T41 amendment to R14.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10 (T51):* **standalone's hint is the task's leading question `leading_question`, word for word, at the root of the task after `unknown`; an overlay file may write it anew with the field `leading_question`.** Format 0.4 gave a closed field, which `api` gives on "Hint" before the first wrong answer, and `task.no-leaks` looks for keys in it (R108 spec; the 2026-11-08 amendment to R82 spec; [format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 14, 19 and 20). The mark "hint is not carried" is no more.
- **All 311 word for word**, and the 53 instructions that do not end in "?" too: they break the task down, as the format wants, and "question" is the field's name. The author's decision 2026-11-10 in the planning of T51, by the recommendation; rejected were rewriting the instructions as questions — 53 edits of standalone's text — and not taking them.
- **The key of a choice and of a time** is looked for in the question by the converter itself — by the same search as in the explanations, a time also with a leading zero, — and in the question, as in `simpler_case`, without exceptions for a retelling of the statement: the question comes before the first error and gives away more than a technique does. One found is a refusal: "its leading question names its key … rewrite it in the leading_question of its overlay"; the run writes the question, names it in the report and counts it in the summary. The format does not do this: it looks nowhere for the key of a tapped choice or for a key that the statement shows, like a word from the question ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20).
- **The key of a number and of a fraction** is looked for by `task.no-leaks`, as in R20, — except a key that the statement shows: the format looks for it nowhere, because a question that names it shows no more than the statement. So `game-56-d2-1` ("1, 2 or 3 stones" with the key 1 and the same numbers in the statement), `game-56-d3-3`, `grid-56-d4-2`, `pct-56-d1-1` and `wp-56-d2-1` stayed word for word.
- **The overlay field `leading_question`** stands in the hint's place; an overlay file with it for a task without a hint is a refusal, like `picture_alt` without a picture. A question cannot be removed by an overlay file: a leak is rewritten. Five are rewritten so: in `ord-56-d3-3` and `ord-56-d4-3` the question named the key word — "Dev", "Abby", — and the converter refused, and in `game-56-d3-1`, `game-56-d4-3` and `par-56-d5-2` the key's number, which is not in the statement, stood among the numbers that the hint says to try — "1, 2, 3 or 4", — and `task.no-leaks` found it. The new texts lead the same way without the key, and numbers are not written as words for this (the T41 amendment to R14).

Rejected: looking for the key of a choice in the question only by proofreading by hand — the leak would be found by a person or by no one; letting the key into the question beside another option or in the words of the statement, as in `givens`, — the question does not retell the statement but leads to the answer. — RUN.md, T51; `tools/port/convert.go`, `batch.go`, `standalone.go`, `convert_test.go`; `test/port.test.mjs`; `tools/port/overlay/` — five overlay files; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10 (T52):* **the pictures of the instances of templates are drawn by the port: the mode `--template-pictures` and the recipe `just template-pictures <module> [--check]`, and CI checks them with a step of the job Modules.** Each template of the module whose picture path holds placeholders takes the source `tools/port/template-pictures/<module folder>/<path without pictures/, placeholders and .png>.json` — a picture in standalone's schema, like the pictures of lessons (the T41 amendment), with placeholders `{name}` in the strings, or a list of variants `[{"when": {…}, "picture": {…}}]`, where the picture changes not only by numbers: so the solution of `changes` has five shares at 20% and four at 25%, and the solution of `groups` is without the line "…" when there are three full rows.
- **The instances the port computes itself** — `module-kit` does not list them, and `build` will not pass without the pictures: the parameters by names in the order of their codes, as an odometer, the derived numbers and the constraint — by `starlark.EvalOptions` of `go.starlark.net` `v0.0.0-20261021141710-4f4267fb140d`, the version of the format's dialect ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 23 and 24). The dependency is BSD-3-Clause, in the list `licenses-allowed`. The count is checked by `test/templates.test.mjs` against the package of `module-kit build`: each instance has its own file, and there are no other files of this path.
- **A placeholder** is only a parameter or a derived number; a result of the solver is a refusal, as is a source that no template names, and a variant that does not fit an instance. Extra files of the templates' paths in `media/pictures/` the mode deletes, and with `--check` names; it does not touch the pictures of tasks, as `port` does not touch the pictures of instances.
- **Time** — 857 pictures are drawn and checked in ≈ 47 s.

Rejected: a parsing of expressions of our own in place of `go.starlark.net` — a second dialect, which could diverge from the format; the numbers of the instances from the package of `module-kit` — the package cannot be built without the pictures; a source per instance — 483 data files instead of six. — RUN.md, T52; `tools/port/templates.go`, `picture.go`, `main.go`, `templates_test.go`; `justfile`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `go.mod`; CLAUDE.md, Environment; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.2 and 3.5.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10 (T53):* **the source of a template's picture may be a function of its solver: `{"solver": "<function>"}`.** The port executes the program of the template's solver once in the format's dialect — the options `Set`, `While`, `TopLevelControl` and `GlobalReassign`, without `Recursion`, a budget of 100,000,000 steps per template ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 24) — and on each instance calls the named function with a frozen dictionary of the numbers by names in the order of their codes, and `print` writes nothing. For the picture of the statement the dictionary has the same numbers that `solve` gets: the numbers whose placeholders stand in the open fields that section 24 names — so the picture of the statement is exactly what `solve` reads. For the picture of the solution, which `solve` does not read, it has all the parameters and derived numbers. The function returns a picture in standalone's schema. The port encodes it with `go.starlark.net/lib/json` — a module of the same dependency — and draws it the same way as a picture from a file: the same names of members, the same kinds. This is how the templates are drawn whose standalone solver reads the picture (the T53 amendment to R22): `solve` builds the figure with the same function and computes on it. A refusal: the template has no solver; there is no such function; it returns not a dictionary, or a dictionary that JSON does not hold; the program does not execute — `load`, recursion, the budget; the source has other members. Rejected: variants `when` for each instance — 26 pictures of lattices and sticks by hand, which nothing checks against the solver. — RUN.md, T53; `tools/port/templates.go`, `main.go`, `templates_test.go`.

**R13. The first batch of `novatrail/olympiad-math` lies in `modules/olympiad-math/`, version `0.1.0`. The tasks are written by the converter (R12) from the overlay files `tools/port/overlay/`. The manifest, the graph, the catalogs and the solving steps are entries of the format examples `olympiad-math` word for word. `NOTICE` carries standalone's MIT and a note that the content of the prototype under Apache-2.0 is changed. There are no lessons yet — T13.** The author's answers 2026-11-06 in the planning of T12 (RUN.md, T12, «Решено автором»): the version by the recommendation, and the executor does the whole task.
- **The version** — `0.1.0`. While the format is v0, a module's move to a new format version is the major part of its version, and in 0.y.z y plays it ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27). The next format transition will give `0.2.0`, not `2.0.0`, and the new tasks of E3 the minor part within the line.
- **The parts of the module** — by [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6:
  - the example's manifest, except for `age_categories: [under_13, adult]` (R76 spec), the absence of `dependencies` — the batch's skills need no anchor skills — and a header of its own;
  - the graph — three skills and the link `pigeonhole-basic` → `combinatorics-enumeration`;
  - the traps — the 10 that the batch's tasks name; the techniques — 4; the solving steps — all four.

  The entries are taken from the format examples word for word, as text, and the header comment of each file is its own, of `solving-steps.yaml` too. The MIT notice stands in `NOTICE`, not in the comments: comments do not get into the package (the 2026-10-28 amendment to R46 spec).
- **The overlay files** of 27 tasks — `givens`, `unknown` and `simpler_case`, in English, in their own words:
  - `givens` retell the statement and do not name inferred numbers — neither the 366 days of a year nor the 12 months, — and `simpler_case` is a task simpler than the same task, not the first step of the solution ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14). There is no key in them, and `task.no-leaks` holds this;
  - the explanations that give the key away are rewritten. Two named it as a number, as [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6, predicted: `enum-12-d1-3`, option 5, — now "Write the numbers down in order, starting with 11, and count them again: none is left over.", and `pig-56-d5-1`, option 10, — "… Count again how many different numbers of friends a girl can have, from 0 up to the number of boys.". Three more gave it away otherwise, and the review found them: `enum-12-d1-3`, option 3, listed all four numbers; `gaps-56-d1-2`, option 10, ended with the calculation of the key "10 × 5 metres"; `pig-56-d1-1`, option 19, named the key by the ordinal "20th". Their new texts are in the overlay files.
- **The pairs with coinciding numbers** of section 3.6 are reread. The explanation of option 10 of `pig-12-d3-1` — "10 balls could be all the red and blue ones" — is rewritten without the number: the next task of the lesson, `pig-12-d5-1`, asks the same in the same words, and its key is 10 balls. Three pairs are left: the 16 badge swaps of `enum-56-d1-1`, the 19 people per day of `pig-56-d1-1` and the 11 numbers with one last digit of `pig-56-d3-2` are intermediate counts of their own task, and they do not read as the answer of the next one.
- **`NOTICE`** — by section 3.5 ibid.:
  - a line about standalone's content and the text of MIT with "Copyright (c) 2026 MathTrail";
  - a paragraph saying that part of the tasks and Pólya's steps came from the prototype under Apache-2.0 and are changed here — section 4(b) of Apache-2.0. The copy of Apache-2.0 is carried by the module's `LICENSE`.

— *Why:* the version — the argument above. The entries of the format examples have already passed the check of `spec` and been compared with standalone ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4), so the batch takes them as they are.

— *Rejected:*
- `1.0.0`: each transition of format v0 would give a new major — `2.0.0`, `3.0.0`, — though the module is not stable yet;
- the MIT notice in the comments of the files, as in the format examples: it would not get into the package;
- changing the numbers in three pairs of the four: they do not read as the answer of the next task.

— *Consequence:* CI checks the module on every pull request — the tree, `check`, `build` and `diff --first`. [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3, 3.1 and 3.7; README.md — the status. — RUN.md, T12; `modules/olympiad-math/`; `tools/port/overlay/`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07:* **the batch's lessons exist — R14, in the same version `0.1.0`.** The words "There are no lessons yet — T13" above speak of the batch before T13.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T22):* **the module's version in epic E3 is `0.1.1`.** It is set by the first content task, T22, and it does not change until the release of T42: a version that is not in a release has nothing to be compared with (R14). While the format is v0, new tasks in 0.y.z are the minor part, and z plays it ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27); `module-kit diff` against `v0.1.0` accepts it. The module's catalogs are still entries of the format examples word for word, but now those that the tasks of the whole module name, in the example's order: since T22, 11 traps of 20 and 4 techniques of 11. — RUN.md, T22.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T26):* **one entry of a catalog is not word for word.** The `trap_advice` of the trap `wrong-parity` in the format example is "Check parity on small numbers first, such as 1 + 3 and 3 + 5, then say the rule out loud.". `task.no-leaks` looks in it for the keys of each task that names the trap, and four tasks of `parity-alternation` have the key 1, 3 or 5. The text of the whole module SHOULD hold no numbers ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20), so the module writes "Check the rule on a few small sums first, then say it out loud.": without numbers and without the words "odd" and "even", which are themselves keys of tasks that name the trap. Rejected: rewriting the tasks' explanations — the trap advice is not their text; not naming the trap at these tasks — their wrong answer is exactly this trap. The request to fix the example is В25 in table 3 of RUN.md. — RUN.md, T26.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T28):* **and a second one: the `trap_advice` of the trap `time-unit-mixup`.** In the format example it is "Draw a clock face and count on in minutes, remembering that an hour has 60 of them, not 100.", and the task `clk-12-d5-4`, which names the trap, has the key 100. By the same argument as above, the module writes "Draw a clock face and count on in minutes, remembering how many minutes an hour has.". The request to fix the example is В26 in table 3 of RUN.md. — RUN.md, T28.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (R23):* **the trap catalog is again the format example word for word.** Since `spec` v0.9.9 the example `olympiad-math` writes the advice of `wrong-parity` and `time-unit-mixup` in the words of amendments T26 and T28, and the `skills` and descriptions of `part-whole-swap` and `percent-wrong-base` by standalone's tasks at 42b4408 (В25–В27), and the module took these two entries. — R23.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10 (T54), by the executor, on the author's instruction:* **the solving steps are again the format example word for word, with descriptions.** In format 0.4 the example `olympiad-math` gave a description `description` to two steps of the four: "Understand the task" — "Read the task again, and tick what is known and what you are to find.", "Check the answer" — "Put your answer back into the task, and see whether everything fits." (decision 3 of epic E7). The entries of `solving-steps.yaml` are these four steps, as in the example, and the header is its own, with the example's words that a description says how to go by the step and carries no numbers. `module-kit diff` calls the edit a patch. — RUN.md, T54.

**R14. The lessons of the first batch are nine files `modules/olympiad-math/lessons/<skill>-grades-<band>.yaml`, by [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6, and R07. The texts are from the topic pages of standalone's site at 89986bc, word for word, except for the words about the child — the page writes for a parent, and the lesson is read by the learner, — and one step of an example, which solved a task of the module. `NOTICE` names the topic pages. The module's version stays `0.1.0`.** RUN.md, T13: a task without the planning mode, and the texts are proofread by the author.
- **The segments** — in the order of R07, each from the fields of the page `site/content/en/topics/<page>.yaml`:
  1. an explanation: `hero.lead` as a paragraph, then each card `basis` as a paragraph "<title>. <text>". The lesson has no heading of the section `basis`;
  2. a worked example of its own level: `question` — `statement`; a sheet `handwriting_sheet` with `grid: squares`, without `grid_step`; the demo — each step of `steps` as a paragraph and `answer` as a paragraph "Answer: …";
  3. an explanation: the example's note as a paragraph "<title>. <text>", and for a title that is a question, without the full stop, — in seven examples of nine, while "Flags of two stripes" and "Sweets in a jar" have none;
  4. the lesson's three tasks by the table of lessons of the same section 3.6;
  5. an explanation: `method.title` as a paragraph, then each step of `method.steps` as a paragraph with a full stop at the end.

  The title is the page's title and the band's words: "…: first steps", "…: going further", "…: olympiad level". The lesson's skill is its skill, and there is no `age_categories`: the lesson suits all the categories of the manifest, as its tasks do.
- **The words about the child** are only in `hero.lead`, and they are rewritten to address the learner: "a child counts" — "you count", "the first tool a child has" — "the first tool", "a child proves" — "you prove", "It is the first meeting with reasoning about the worst case" — "It is reasoning about the worst case". The module's learner is an adult too (`adult`, R76 spec). The note "30 is the number children forget" is left: it speaks of children in general, not of the reader.
- **No numbers are added.** The steps of the method go without numbers: each page has three of them, and the format's search for numbers would read "3." as the number 3 ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20) — the key of `pig-34-d1-2` in the lesson `pigeonhole-basic-grades-3-4`. The texts of the pages carry the same numbers that the rule of choosing tasks checked ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6), and `lesson.no-leaks` finds no violations. That the rule in `module-kit` works is checked: a copy of the module with the key 14 in the demo of the lesson `counting-gaps-grades-1-2` was rejected.
- **The example's step "No two 2s side by side"** of the lesson `combinatorics-enumeration-grades-5-6` is rewritten. On the page it listed the two-digit numbers of 1 and 2 — "11, 12 and 21, three, since 22 is not allowed", — that is, all four numbers of the task `enum-12-d1-3` and its solution, and an example must not solve a task of the module ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21). Now it is "Two digits: three numbers, since two 2s may not stand side by side." `lesson.no-leaks` does not catch this: it looks in a lesson only for the keys of its own tasks, and `enum-12-d1-3` stands in the lesson of band 1–2.
- **The pair that is left:** the card "In a row, one fewer" — "six trees, five gaps" — stands before `gaps-12-d3-1` in the lesson `counting-gaps-grades-1-2`, where there are 6 trees on each side of the path. The card names the topic's rule on the page's numbers, and the task asks about two rows: its key 10 is not in the lesson, and 5 is its trap value `stopped-early`, the answer of whoever counted one side. That is what the lesson is for: it teaches the rule before the task. If the author decides otherwise in proofreading, the card's number changes in all three lessons `counting-gaps`.
- **`NOTICE`** names the texts of the topic pages of standalone's site too: the lessons carry them, and in the package the MIT notice stands only there (the 2026-10-28 amendment to R46 spec).
- **The version.** There are no releases of `modules` yet, so `0.1.0` is published nowhere, and the lessons go into it: the first published `0.1.0` will come out with them already (T16).

— *Why:* a part of a lesson's explanation is a paragraph of text or a picture, and the format knows neither headings nor tables ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 14 and 21), so the title of a card and of a note stands at the start of its paragraph. The learner reads the lesson themselves, and a text about them in the third person — "a child counts" — would read as a text for someone else.

— *Rejected:*
- the card's title as a paragraph of its own: the app would show it as a line of text, not as a heading;
- the texts for a parent word for word — the argument above;
- the table `idea` and its note: the format knows no tables, and the lines "6 − 1 = 5", "7 + 1 = 8" do not read without the table; the gist and the rules of the topic are already carried by the cards `basis`;
- `method.lead`: it speaks of "the examples below" on the page; `traps`, `home` and `description` are for a parent; `art` is the captions of drawings, and there are no pictures in the lessons (R07);
- the numbers of the method's steps — the argument above;
- the example's step word for word: the example would solve `enum-12-d1-3` for a learner to whom the engine gives the lesson of 5–6 before this task;
- `0.1.1` or `0.2.0` for the lessons: a version that is not in a release is compared with nothing.

— *Consequence:* `module-kit check` passes without violations or warnings, and so does `just modules-check`. [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3, 3.1 and 3.7; README.md — the status. — RUN.md, T13; `modules/olympiad-math/lessons/`, `NOTICE`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T38), by the executor, on the author's instruction:* **the lessons of epic E3 are built the same way, but from the topic pages at 42b4408, and their tasks are chosen by the rule of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6, without points 1 and 5.** Points 1 and 5 let into the first batch only tasks with a number answer and without a picture, except `row`. Now the module has tasks of any kind of answer and with pictures of all kinds, and a lesson takes at difficulties 1, 3 and 5 the first task by ID of its skill and band — other than a template — whose key does not stand in the lesson's text or in the statement of an earlier task of the lesson and does not equal that task's key. A number key is looked for by value, as in point 4; a key with a word in it — "Kim", "15 March", "It can never be done" — as a phrase, case-insensitively and with word boundaries. The preference of point 3 — a task whose explanations of the wrong options do not name the key — changes nothing in E3: the port has already rewritten such explanations (R05). Templates do not go into a lesson: their keys change from attempt to attempt. As in R14, the texts of a page that address a parent or a drawing are rewritten for the learner — "For many children…", "As the drawing shows" — and so are the texts that solved a task of the module: the question "Can 15 children stand in a ring…" with the card "Taking turns" solved `par-34-d2-3`, and the chain "Ann taller than Ben, Ben taller than Kate" of the card "A chain goes on" solved the lesson's task `ord-12-d1-1`. The pairs of a lesson's tasks where the key of a later one stands in the explanations or the solution of an earlier one are reread, as in R13, and listed in «Сделано» of the task. Rejected: keeping point 1 — the lessons of `logic-ordering` would be left almost without tasks: the skill has a word answer in two thirds of its tasks; taking the first task without checking the keys — `lesson.no-leaks` looks for the tasks' keys in the lesson's text. — RUN.md, T38; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T39), by the executor, on the author's instruction:* **a key phrase is looked for without quotation marks and without a sign at the end, and a task whose solution gives the answer of a later task of the lesson the rule skips, and «Сделано» records this.** The key of `kl-34-d1-1` is "I am a liar." with a full stop, while the page writes “I am a liar” in quotation marks, and the search for the phrase with the full stop did not find it, though the lesson analysed this phrase right before the task. The solution of `wp-34-d3-1` lists six of the seven states of the answer of `wp-34-d5-2`, and both have the same `simpler_case`: the rule of keys catches a number, not a chain of states, so such a task is skipped by the executor, who names it with the reason. As in amendment T38, the texts of a page that began to solve a task of the module are rewritten: the question "how do you measure exactly 4 litres" with jugs of 5 and 3 — this is `wp-34-d2-1` — is now about jugs of 8 and 5 and 1 litre, and the card of states is "0 and 5, then 5 and 0". The page's markup, `**…**`, does not go into the lesson's text: a paragraph of the format is plain text. Rejected: looking for chains of states and other traces of a solution in the lesson's text by a program — their form is different for each topic, while such pairs are rare and visible on reading. — RUN.md, T39; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T41), by the executor, on the author's instruction:* **the lessons `counting-figures` and `geometry-cubes-paper` take from the topic pages the introduction, the headings of the cards and the method, and the cards and examples are rewritten: the pages of these topics were written around the module's tasks. The pictures of the lessons are the site's drawings, `lattice`, which the converter draws (the T41 amendment to R12), and where the text is rewritten, drawings for the new text. If no fitting task is left at a later difficulty, the rule takes at an earlier one the next in its order, and the lesson's tasks go by difficulty.** Decided in the planning of T41:
- **the pages solved the module's tasks.** The cards gave the keys of `fig-12-d1-1`, `fig-12-d2-1`, `fig-12-d3-1`, `cube-12-d1-1` and the course of solution of `cube-12-d2-1` and `cube-12-d5-1` — "A square of 2 by 2 cells holds 4 small squares and 1 big square", "Two lines from corner to corner make 8 triangles in a square", "A row of 3 squares takes 10 matches", "1 and 6, 2 and 5", "folded once has 2 layers… and folded again 4". Now the cards state the same rule without numbers.
