The examples "Two lines from the top", "Matches to take away", "A die rolls on" and "Two folds and a punch" are the tasks `fig-12-d5-1`, `fig-56-d4-1`, `cube-34-d5-1` and `cube-12-d5-1`, and "The face opposite" asks "opposite 2" with the answer 4, like `cube-12-d3-1`, — about the second cell of the column, whose opposite face is the course of solution of `cube-12-d4-1` and `cube-34-d4-1`. Now the examples have other figures or numbers: three rays instead of two, a rectangle 2 by 3 of matches instead of a square 2 by 2, a die with 1 on top instead of 2, faces with letters and a question about the top cell of the column, and the punch strikes at the point where the folds meet. The example 3–4 of `counting-figures`, a square 3 by 3, solved no task, but its numbers 3 and 4 are the keys of three of the five tasks of the band, and the lesson would be left with two tasks; now it is a rectangle 2 by 5. The note "After four rolls" of the example 3–4 of `geometry-cubes-paper` is removed: a period of four rolls is the course of solution of `cube-56-d2-1` and `cube-56-d5-1`. Each answer of a new example is checked by exhaustive search;
- **the example 5–6 of `geometry-cubes-paper`** is the second example of band 1–2, "Two folds and a punch", raised to 5–6: the punch strikes at the point where the folds meet, and there is one hole. Rejected: the example 3–4 — the lesson 3–4 already takes it; a lesson without an example — R14 gives every lesson an example; the same example with two, three folds — 4 and 8 holes, the keys of `cube-12-d5-1` and `cube-56-d4-1`;
- **the pictures.** The site's drawings (`web/src/site/art/`) are data `lattice`, of the same schema as the pictures of standalone's tasks, and the converter draws them. A lesson shows a picture in the statement of an example, and the example 5–6 of `counting-figures` also the removed matches in the demo. Rejected: pictures of the converter from a description without the site's data — the same `lattice`, only without a source;
- **backtracking.** In `geometry-cubes-paper` 1–2 three tasks of the five have the key 4, and the key 2 of `cube-12-d2-1` stands in the statement of `cube-12-d3-1`, "opposite 2". The greedy choice took `cube-12-d3-1` at difficulty 3 and found nothing for difficulty 5. With backtracking it takes `cube-12-d4-1`, and at 5 `cube-12-d2-1`, and the lesson names them by difficulty. This does not change the lessons of T38–T40: there the greedy choice found three tasks. Rejected: excluding a task by the executor's word, as in T39 — there the solution gave the answer, while here the task fits, and the choice decides the order;
- **the rule does not look at the `alt` of the pictures of the statement of an earlier task, as in T38–T40:** the picture of `cube-12-d1-1` is a face with the number 2, and 2 is the key of `cube-12-d2-1`, the number of holes. With `alt` the lesson would have not one fitting triple, and this is a coincidence, not an answer; the pair is listed in «Сделано» of T41.

Rejected: leaving the pages as they are — the lesson would solve the module's tasks, while by the format an example SHOULD NOT ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21); writing numbers as words so that the rule does not see them — the new texts do without counting where a digit would coincide with the key of a task of the lesson, and the words of the page itself, like "two squares in a row with one square between them", stay. — RUN.md, T41; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.5 and 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10 (T54), by the executor, on the author's instruction:* **the steps of the method are a part `numbered_list` of the explanation after the paragraph "How to solve: …", as in the format example `the-worst-luck.yaml`.** The point "No numbers are added" of R14 wrote the steps as paragraphs without numbers: the search for numbers read the numeral "3." as the number 3 (request В21). Format 0.4 gave a lesson's explanation a numbered list: the app draws the numerals, and the search for numbers does not see them ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 20 and 21; R111 spec). So in each of the 41 lessons the three paragraphs of the steps became one part `numbered_list`, and the texts did not change: the leak check looks in the items of the list for the same as in paragraphs. Rejected: numbers in the texts of the steps — "1. Find…" — the search for numbers reads them as numbers. — RUN.md, T54; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6.

## School maths for grade 3

**R15. The set of school maths for grade 3 is the module `novatrail/grade-3-math` 0.1.0 in `modules/grade-3-math/`, on a workbook. It has four skills of the graph of the format example — `times-tables`, `division-with-remainder`, `fractions-of-a-whole` and `comparing-fractions`, — 12 pages, three per skill, of difficulties 1, 3 and 5, and a lesson per skill. The explanation language is English, the audience `under_13`. The texts are its own, and there is no `NOTICE`.** The author's answers 2026-11-07 in the planning of T14 (RUN.md, T14, «Решено автором»), all four by the recommendations, and the rest by the plan that the author approved. The make-up is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.8.
- **Skills.** Four skills of the graph of the example `school-math-grade-3`, word for word and closed under prerequisites. They teach all three anchor skills that the slice names: `core.multiplication-table`, `core.division-with-remainder` and `core.fractions-of-a-whole` (R50 spec). The tiers are 1, 2, 2 and 3, without names, as in the example. There are no prerequisites that are anchor skills, so there is no `dependencies` either.
- **Check kinds** — all that the format gives a workbook, except `predicate`:
  - `number` with a box anywhere in the line;
  - two answer slots in one line — the quotient and the remainder;
  - `fraction` with `form: any` and with `form: reduced`;
  - `choice` by hand — the signs `<`, `>` and `=`.

  The page's statement names the form of the answer and the signs that the box takes: the options of the check are closed (R56 spec).
- **Pages.** 12 files `tasks/<skill>-d<difficulty>.yaml`, 6–10 lines each, in one band `grade-3`. Each page fits by the layout of `canvas` v0.1.2: from 3 to 7 rows ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4).
  - Story lines are long items with `units`. They have `simpler_case` on smaller numbers: none of these numbers, nor what comes out of them, is the key of its line, and what is written there is not a key of the page. The fractions in `simpler_case` and in the stories name the page's keys neither in digits nor in words: the search for numbers does not read words ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20).
  - Trap values are at typical wrong answers, and their texts are without numbers. In a division line with two answer slots the trap "the quotient one too small" is named only by the quotient's answer slot: the remainder that comes out of the same error is the same error, and it is not counted twice.
  - A line does not repeat a fact that already stands in the text of this page or in the solution of an earlier page of the same lesson: otherwise the answer is seen before it is solved ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20). So the facts of the stories and of the counting at d5 are other than at d1 and d3.
- **Catalogs.** Traps — 11:
  - word for word from the example — three: `times-table-slip`, `remainder-too-big` and `not-in-lowest-terms`;
  - `wrong-operation` from the example, but with other words. Lines without a story catch it too, "3 × 4 = [ ]", while the example's description and advice spoke of a story — "where the story multiplies", "Draw the bags and the apples";
  - `reversed-comparison` from the example, but with the skill `comparing-fractions`: the example's skill `numbers-to-1000` is not in the set, and the entry word for word would break `traps.known-skills`;
  - its own — six, each for an error that the example does not have: `took-away` (subtracted where one multiplies or divides), `remainder-left-out`, `remainder-not-added`, `top-and-bottom-swapped`, `one-part-only` and `greater-bottom-greater-fraction`.

  A trap value names only the trap whose error it shows: at the answer slot "the greater of two fractions" a wrong fraction has no trap, since no sign is written there. Techniques — three from the example and its own `draw-the-parts`; solving steps — the example's two steps.
- **Lessons** — `lessons/<skill>.yaml`: an explanation, then the skill's three pages by increasing difficulty. There is no worked example. The texts of the lessons, traps and techniques have no digits: the pages' keys are small numbers, and the leak check searches for them in all these texts. There are no signs of comparison there either, though the check does not search for them — below. That the rule works is checked: a copy of the module with "47 shared by 6" in the lesson `division-with-remainder` was rejected — `lesson.no-leaks`, the key 6 of the page `division-with-remainder-d3`.
- **The check does not search for a sign in a lesson.** A key of `choice` that stands in a text seen together with the item is not searched for ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20). The statement of a comparison page names all three signs, so a copy of a lesson with the sign `<` passes the check. This is intended: a sign that the learner sees in the statement does not give the answer away.
- **Language** — English, as in the format example and the two other modules of the slice. This module did not close О-2, so a row «в реестр spec» goes to О-2 (RUN.md, table 3).
- **Audience** — `under_13`, grade 3, as R41 spec and the example have it.
- **Version** — `0.1.0`, by the argument of R13.

— *Why:*
- **Four skills**: the smallest graph that teaches all three anchor skills of the slice and gives all the check kinds of a workbook. A skill without tasks gives the engine nothing (R07), and seven skills would almost double the text to proofread.
- **Three pages per skill**: within a skill the engine has something to choose from, as in the first batch (R07).
- **Lessons**: without them `lesson.no-leaks` is not checked on a workbook, and the criterion of product 10 spec «проходит уроки до конца» ("goes through the lessons to the end") would wait.
- **Without a worked example**: in format 0.3 an example exists only on a sheet, and an example on a sheet in a workbook module would show the learner another canvas.
- **Without `predicate` and templates**: whether `predicate` stays in v1 is decided by `spec` (R56 spec), and templates on a workbook are already checked by the format example `division-with-remainder`. The module would take them for the format's sake, not for the learner's.
- **English**: the same module in Russian is another module ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10), and «47 : 6 = [ ] (ост. [ ])» is already a long item by the format's guideline.
- **Only `under_13`**: the module is not part of the first application. Adding a category later is a minor version, and removing one a major ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27).

— *Rejected:*
- the whole graph of the example, 7 skills; 3 skills without `comparing-fractions`;
- two pages per skill; a set without lessons;
- a worked example on a sheet;
- `predicate` and templates;
- pictures of fractions: one more kind of drawing would be needed, and fractions in words check the same;
- Russian;
- `adult` in the audience;
- `reversed-comparison` word for word, with `numbers-to-1000`; `wrong-operation` word for word, on lines without a story;
- a trap on both answer slots of a division line for one error.

— *Consequence:*
- `module-kit check` passes without violations or warnings, and so does `just modules-check`: `diff --first` for both modules;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md): sections 2, 3, 3.1, 3.7, the new 3.8 and 4; README.md — the status;
- RUN.md, table 3 — a row «в реестр spec» to О-2: the explanations of all three modules of the slice are in English;
- for T17 — what the set asks of the format:
  - a worked example on a workbook, which 0.3 does not have;
  - a rule of the page by the layout of `canvas`, which T38 `spec` prepares anyway.

— RUN.md, T14; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.8; `modules/grade-3-math/`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10 (T55), by the executor, on the author's instruction:* **in each lesson, after the explanation, there is a worked example on a workbook, like the format example `remainders-on-a-workbook.yaml`: lines with boxes, their values and the demo `demo`, which fills each box once. The module's version is `0.3.0`.** Format 0.4 let a worked example onto a workbook ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21; R111 spec) — the answer to the request В19, and the point "Without a worked example" of R15 no longer holds. The lines and the values of the boxes carry numbers that the leak check does not look for, — and no line of the example coincides with a line of a page of the lesson, and no fact of the example stands in any page of the lesson, so that the example does not solve a task of the module: `[7] × 3 = 21`, `18 ÷ 2 = [9]` and `8 × [5] = 40`; `26 ÷ 3 = [8] r [2]` and `34 ÷ 6 = [5] r [4]`; `1/3 of 15 = [5]` and `3/4 of 12 = [9]`; `2/9 [<] 5/9` and `1/4 [>] 1/9`. The statement and the texts of the demo are without digits, like all the texts of the lessons, and for the comparison of fractions also without signs of comparison; the numbers in them are in words. `lesson.example-fills`, `lesson.workbook-page` and `lesson.no-leaks` pass, and on a copy of the module each of the three rules fires: an unfilled box, a value wider than its box and a line equal to a line of a page. The version is `0.3.0`, not the `0.2.1` that the task's plan expected: the example stands as the second segment, the lesson's pages shift, and `module-kit diff` calls this a major edit (`module-version.step`); an example at the end of the lesson, after the pages, would have left the edit minor, but would show the method when the pages are already solved. The texts are proofread by the author. — RUN.md, T55; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.8; `modules/grade-3-math/lessons/`.

## Short essays

**R16. The set of short essays is the module `novatrail/english-essays` 0.1.0 in `modules/english-essays/`, on a lined handwriting sheet. It has all six skills of the graph of the format example `short-essays`, 8 tasks — three choices of a first sentence and five essays with a rubric, one of them only for adults, — and 6 lessons, one of them with a worked example. The audience is all four age categories, without grades. The topics for learners under 18 are made up and general, and the statement of each essay ends with a reminder not to write names. The explanation language is English, the texts are its own and those of the format examples, and there is no `NOTICE`.** The author's answers 2026-11-07 in the planning of T15 (RUN.md, T15, «Решено автором»), all four by the recommendations, and the rest by the plan that the author approved. The make-up is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.9.
- **Skills** — the graph of the example `short-essays` word for word: `reading-model-essays` (`none`), `choosing-a-first-sentence` (`verdict`), `writing-a-first-sentence`, `paragraphs`, `endings` and `improving-an-essay` (`rubric`). The tiers are 1, 1, 2, 3, 4 and 4, with the example's names. The graph is not a chain: tier 1 has two skills, and `endings` and `improving-an-essay` both wait for `paragraphs`. Between their lessons the engine's rule chooses, and the graph links the lesson `reading-model-essays` to no other, and its place too is chosen by the engine's rule ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21). There are no prerequisites that are anchor skills, so there is no `dependencies` either.
- **Check kinds** — both that the format gives writing:
  - `choice` by tapping — in the three tasks of choosing a first sentence, in the band `first-steps`, difficulties 1, 3 and 5. The key stands among the options and is not searched for, so `leak_phrases` holds it, as in the example ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20). Each wrong option has a trap value, and all but the one of `announcing-the-topic` in `best-first-sentence-1` have a trap text. That is the option of the example's entry word for word, and after it the learner reads the trap advice;
  - `rubric` with `binding: sheet` — in the five essays, three criteria each. The criteria are about structure and content, and none is about capital letters, spelling or punctuation: in handwriting a capital is told from a small letter for c, o, s, u, v, w, x and z only by height (R71 spec).

  There is an item with a verdict, so the manifest has the example's `rung_templates` (`manifest.rung-templates`).
- **Tasks.** From the format examples — `best-first-sentence-1` word for word, `undiscovered-island-1` with the reminder and `a-day-you-remember-1` with the reminder and a third criterion that asks for no names. Its own — `best-first-sentence-2` and `-3`, `talking-umbrella-1` on `writing-a-first-sentence`, `weather-shop-1` on `paragraphs` and `dragon-draft-1` on `improving-an-essay`: its statement has a draft with repetitions, which the learner writes anew.
- **Topics and names** (П-20; privacy 3.2 spec). For all categories under 18 the topics are made up and general, without family, friends, home and school: an island that nobody has found, a talking umbrella, a weather shop, a dragon. There is one task about the learner's life — `a-day-you-remember-1`, with `age_categories: [adult]`, and its lesson `writing-about-a-day` has the same. The statement of each essay ends with the paragraph "Do not write your name or the real name of anyone you know.", that of the task for adults too: П-20 recommends the reminder for an adult as well. The example's criterion "It names at least one person or place of that day" argues with the reminder, so here it is "It tells of at least one person or place of that day".
- **Catalogs.** Traps — two from the example word for word and its own `giving-it-all-away`: a first sentence that tells the whole story at once. The techniques and the solving steps are the example's entries word for word.
- **Lessons** — six: `reading-model-essays` from the example word for word; `writing-about-a-day` from the example, but with the skill `endings`, not `[paragraphs, endings]`; `first-sentences` — the example's explanations, the three choice tasks and `talking-umbrella-1`; and `paragraphs`, `endings` and `improving-an-essay` — its own, an explanation and an essay. A lesson's skill is not a prerequisite, yet it puts the lesson in order: with `paragraphs` among its skills, `writing-about-a-day` would go only after `first-sentences`, and an adult could write about a day of their life before the lessons about paragraphs and endings. With `endings` alone the lesson goes after `paragraphs`, and `lesson.task-skills` holds: `endings` is the primary skill of its task. In the lesson `improving-an-essay` there is a worked example on a lined sheet between them: the demo corrects its own draft step by step, not the draft of the lesson's task ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21). The texts of the lessons have no `leak_phrases` of the choice tasks. That the rule works is checked: a copy of the module with "went dark" in the lesson `first-sentences` was rejected — `lesson.no-leaks`, a phrase of the task `best-first-sentence-2`.
- **Audience** — `under_13`, `from_13_to_consent`, `from_consent_to_18` and `adult`, without grades, as R41 spec and the example have it. The manifest of 0.3 names no presentation style (О-23), so one text serves both a child and an adult: short simple sentences.
- **Language** — English (R41 spec). **Version** — `0.1.0`, by the argument of R13.

— *Why:*
- **All six skills**: the example's graph is small and already gives all three kinds of `assessability` and a fork. Without `improving-an-essay` the last tier would be left with one skill: there would be no two lessons with a rubric after one and the same lesson, between which the engine's rule chooses, and the set would lose its only skill of correcting one's own text.
- **An essay per skill with a rubric**: such skills have the author's order, and a second essay gives the engine nothing, while there would be half as much again to proofread. Three choice tasks are for the skill with a verdict: outside the lessons, when the engine gives a task for practice, it has something to choose from, as in the two other modules (R07, R15). In the lesson `first-sentences` they go in a row, in the author's order: the lesson has a skill with a rubric too, and the engine neither adds segments nor skips them ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21).
- **One task for adults**: so the `age_categories` of a task and of a lesson are checked by a module of the slice, not only by the example, and all this is by the recommendation of П-20: for an adult, any topics.
- **The reminder in the statement**: until the answer to П-20 there is no one else to show it, and the learner sees the statement before writing. Whether the platform shows it itself is decided by `spec` — a row «в реестр spec» (RUN.md, table 3).
- **The worked example**: `worked_example` on a second subject is what T38 `spec` checks on writing. On a workbook there is none in 0.3 (R15), and here the canvas is a sheet.

— *Rejected:*
- two essays per skill with a rubric, 12 tasks; five skills, without `improving-an-essay`;
- only made-up topics for all, without a task for adults;
- the reminder without a row «в реестр spec»; the row without the reminder in the module;
- lessons without a worked example;
- criteria about capital letters, spelling and punctuation;
- the third criterion of `a-day-you-remember-1` word for word;
- the lesson's skills of `writing-about-a-day` word for word, `[paragraphs, endings]`: the lesson would go before the lessons about paragraphs and endings.

— *Consequence:*
- `module-kit check` passes without violations or warnings, and so does `just modules-check`: `diff --first` for the three modules. A copy without `rung_templates` is rejected by `manifest.rung-templates`;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md): sections 2, 3, 3.1, 3.7 and the new 3.9; README.md — the status;
- RUN.md, table 3 — a row «в реестр spec» to П-20: the reminder not to write names stands in the module's statements;
- for T17 — what the set asks of the format:
  - the format has no place for a reminder before the work is sent — only the text of the statement;
  - the manifest names no presentation style (О-23), and one text serves all ages;
  - a lesson's own skill does not put it in order, so the lesson `writing-about-a-day` had to be narrowed to `endings`.

— RUN.md, T15; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.9; `modules/english-essays/`.

## The first release

**R17. The releases of `modules` go in the line `0.1`, and the first is `v0.1.0`. Its own `release.yml` calls the shared `release.yml` of `.github` v0.1.15 by SHA with `previous-file: module-index.json` and makes a dry run of the release on every pull request from a branch of the repository, publishing nothing. The recipe `release-files VERSION PREVIOUS` builds all the modules, builds the index with the command `module-kit index` and puts an archive only for a package whose entry names this release; the archive is the same byte for byte on any runner. Д3 is closed when `release-verify` accepts the files of the first release.** The author's answers 2026-11-07 in the planning of T16 (RUN.md, T16, «Решено автором»), all four by the recommendations. The task carries out R03: what the release carries and what its files are called was decided there.
- **The line and the number** — `RELEASE_LINE: "0.1"` in the `env` of `release.yml`, as in the template of architecture 3.3 .github and in `canvas`. The number is the tag of the repository itself, not a version of a module or of the format (R31 spec; R03).
- **`release.yml`** — by the template of architecture 3.3 .github, the shared `release.yml` v0.1.15, commit 89d3cee: `.github` has no newer release, and `previous-file` is there since v0.1.14 (Д3). The list `paths` is from [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3: the template still has no kind `modules` (the 2026-11-06 amendment to R03). The references of `ci.yml` to `.github` need no edit.
- **The dry run on a pull request** — the trigger `pull_request` and `publish: ${{ github.event_name != 'pull_request' }}`, as in `canvas`. The shared release builds the files from `git archive` in the `toolchain` stage, signs and checks them, but does not publish them. The run after CI on `main` goes only if CI is green. A pull request from a fork skips the dry run: it is not given `id-token`, and without it there is no signature. The dry run is not in the ruleset of `main`.
- **The recipe `release-files version previous`** — bash in the root `justfile`:
  - an empty `previous` is `--first`, a file is `--previous`, anything else is code 2; `withdrawals.yaml` is `--withdrawals`, and without it `--no-withdrawals`;
  - the tree `modules/`, the CLI on `PATH` against `MODULE_KIT_VERSION`, `check` and the ID `novatrail/<folder>` of each module are checked by `_modules-check` — one copy of these rules, — without the comparison with the published versions: CI of the same commit does it, and what the release needs of it is held by `index` — `module-index.one-package` and `module-index.version-above`;
  - `build` of each module, then `index` by the form of architecture 3.6 module-kit, in a temporary folder;
  - the archive — for a package whose entry names `VERSION`, after checking `package_digest` against the sha256 of the bytes of `inventory.json` and the make-up of the package: three files and the folder `device/`, nothing more;
  - the root `LICENSE`; `dist/` is put in place whole, and a refusal does not leave it behind. `dist/` is in `.gitignore`.
- **The archive** — GNU tar `--format=gnu --sort=name --owner=0 --group=0 --numeric-owner --mode=u=rwX,go=rX --mtime=@0`, the entries `inventory.json`, `server.json`, `device.json` and `device/`, without `./`, then `gzip -n -9`. `tutor` builds its archive the same way.
- **An unchanged module** does not change its entry and gets no archive. If since the previous release only `justfile` or `release.yml` changed, the release carries the same index and `LICENSE` — R03 has already accepted this price.

— *Why:*
- **The line `0.1`**: the release number must hold no link to the format version — the format is named by the manifest of each module, and the modules of one release may be written in different format versions.
- **The dry run on a pull request**: a release is immutable, and index entries never go away (`module-index.entries-kept`). An error of the archive of the first release would be fixed only by a new version of each module. The tests and a local run go through neither the build from `git archive` with a Dockerfile without context nor the signature, while the dry run goes through them before the merge — on the pull request of T16 and on each next one.
- **A reproducible archive**: nothing holds the bytes of the archive ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3), but a test checks the same bytes on the same tree. So the release's run and a run by hand give one and the same file, and it can be compared with the published one.
- **Checking the digest before the archive**: the archive is named by the entry's digest, and a wrong name would be found by no one but the reader who needs it.
- **Building all the modules**: there is no git in the recipe's tree, and the recipe does not know which module has changed (R03).

— *Rejected:*
- the line `0.3` by the format version — the argument above;
- a plain `tar -czf`: the time and the owner of the build would get into the bytes of the archive;
- only a local dry run — the argument above;
- an archive of each package in each release — copies that no entry names (R03).

— *Consequence:*
- `.github/workflows/release.yml`; `justfile` — `release-files`; `test/release-files.test.mjs`; `.gitignore`;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md): sections 3, 3.1, 3.3, 3.7 and 4; README.md and CLAUDE.md;
- Д3 in table 4 of RUN.md goes when the first release is out and `release-verify` has accepted its index and archives (RUN.md, T16).

— RUN.md, T16; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07:* **the first release is out.** The author merged the pull request of T16, #29, and Release put out `v0.1.0` at 6d061e9, immutable: `module-index.json`, `LICENSE` and the archives of the three packages 0.1.0. `release-verify` accepted all five files, their sha256 matched the local dry run, and `just modules-check` compared the modules with the real index. Д3 is closed. — RUN.md, T16.

## The tag `spec` v0.9.5

**R18. `modules` stands on `spec` v0.9.5, the latest release of `spec`. That release records the answers to the rows «в реестр spec» В16–В18, and the rows left table 3 of RUN.md. The tag closes Т-16: the gate of `sim` in CI of `modules` is everything that the simulator reads from the package, without bands (R103 spec), and R03 got an amendment. The tag changes neither the format, nor the template, nor the container.** The author's instruction 2026-11-07: move to the latest version of `spec`.
- **What the tag decided for `modules`** (`git -C spec diff v0.9.3 v0.9.5`, read 2026-11-07; Д109–Д112 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec` and table 5 there; [COORDINATION.md](../spec/COORDINATION.md), rows 3.6 and 4.5–4.8):
  - **the rows of table 3 of RUN.md** — where the answer to each lay:
    - В16 — the 2026-11-06 amendment to R93 spec: the recipe `sample-check` will have a mode for a module's eval set — the module's ID and the files of its `eval/`, the purpose `PURPOSE_EVAL_SET`, markup, the source `modules` with this ID and different sample IDs. Epic E7 of `spec` writes it, and CI of `modules` calls it on the `eval/` of each module from the tag where the mode comes out, before the first sample in `eval/` (Д109 `spec`). This is epic E4 of table 2 of RUN.md and [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.4;
    - В17 — the 2026-11-07 addition to О-2: the explanations of all three modules of the slice are in English, and О-2 stays about the decimal comma and about the modules after the slice;
    - В18 — the 2026-11-07 addition to П-20: the format has no place for the reminder, except the text of the statement. Who shows it is decided together with П-20, before the beta with children, and until the answer the reminder stands in the statements of `english-essays` (R16);
  - **the gate of `sim`** (R103 spec; Т-16 closed; Д111 `spec`; repositories 4 spec and the card architecture 3.10 spec): `sim` is run when what the simulator reads from the package has changed — this is decided by the command of `sim` "has it changed", — on the pull request's package, with invariants and absolute thresholds, without bands. A pull request that changes the tag of `sim` runs all the modules, and a format transition goes without the simulator. Until the first tag of `sim` there is no gate (R76 spec), and row 4.5 of [COORDINATION.md](../spec/COORDINATION.md) waits, for the first tag, for published packages of `modules` — they exist since `v0.1.0` (R17);
  - **synthetic samples in the eval set** — the 2026-11-07 amendment to R93 spec: a file that the samples page of `canvas` did not write writes `canvas_version` `0.0.0` and `text_set_version` `none`, and `sample-check` requires this of a synthetic sample (Д110 `spec`). There are no samples in the modules' `eval/` yet; this rule is for epic E4;
  - **the release of `modules` in the gate file of `sim`** (R103 spec; the card architecture 3.9 spec): the gate file names the release of `modules` by its tag and the digest of its `module-index.json`, and the packages by their digests. For the releases of `modules` this is one more reader — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 1;
  - R104 spec and the edits of cards 3.5 and 3.8 — the engine's inputs and the first versions of the libraries — are about other components. They do not change the documents of `modules`.
- **What changed here:**
  - `spec/` — at v0.9.5, commit 86f442a. The tag in CLAUDE.md, README.md, the opening of RUN.md and the status of [architecture.md](architecture.md) is v0.9.5. That the repository was at v0.9.3 and earlier is told by the opening of the log and R01, R02, R06 and R09: this is history;
  - the template: `repo-template`, `tools/new-repo`, `.devcontainer`, `justfile`, RULES.md and `format/` did not change from v0.9.3 to v0.9.5 (`git -C spec diff --stat`), so the move requires neither a merge of the template nor a rebuild of the container;
  - the 2026-11-07 amendment to R03 — the gate of `sim`;
  - [architecture.md](architecture.md) — the status, sections 1, 2, 2.1, 3.2, the opening of "How it was decided" in 3.3, 3.4 and 3.7;
  - RUN.md — table 2: E4 has the answer to В16 and the rule of synthetic samples; table 3 — В16–В18 are closed, and В23 «в реестр spec» is new: what the review of a format transition holds; table 4 — Д5 by R103 spec, and Д6 is new: until the eval-set mode of `sample-check` there are no samples in `eval/`.

— *Why now:* the author's instruction 2026-11-07. v0.9.5 is the latest release as of this day, immutable (`gh release view -R altnovatrail/spec`), and `gh release verify v0.9.5 -R altnovatrail/spec` accepted it. Without the move `modules` would write the gate of `sim` by the old text of Т-16, and the rows В16–В18 would hang open, though the answer to them already exists (Д111 `spec`).

- a row in table 1 under a new number: there is no current epic, and the task is the author's instruction, recorded by this decision, as with R09.

— *Consequence:* in table 3 of RUN.md — В19–В22, the requests of the first content to the format, and В23. Д5 waits for the first tag of `sim` and the form of the call of its command "has it changed", and Д6 for a tag of `spec` with the eval-set mode. — RUN.md, tables 2–4; CLAUDE.md; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md); R03.

## The tag `spec` v0.9.8

**R19. `modules` stands on `spec` v0.9.8, the latest release of `spec`. That release records the answers to all the rows «в реестр spec» of this repository — В19–В23, — and no questions are left in table 3 of RUN.md. The rule of the order of lessons and the format example took up what `english-essays` had already done, the review of a format transition keeps unchanged everything that the simulator reads, and R03 got an amendment. The tag changes neither the template nor the container.** The author's instruction 2026-11-08: move to the latest version of `spec`.
- **What the tag decided for `modules`** (`git -C spec diff v0.9.5 v0.9.8`, read 2026-11-08; Д113 and Д115 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec` and table 5 there):
  - **the rows of table 3 of RUN.md** — where the answer to each lay:
    - В19 — epic E6 of `spec`: the rule of a workbook page and a worked example with the canvas `workbook` — the lines of the example with boxes, and the demo fills the boxes step by step, — in one format version. Until it, `grade-3-math` goes without worked examples (R15);
    - В20 — the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21: a lesson that names a skill and its prerequisite goes after the lessons of what the prerequisite waits for, but not after the lesson of the prerequisite itself, and a lesson that must go after the lesson of a skill names only the skills that wait for it. The example `essays-for-adults.yaml` is now `skills: [endings]`, like the lesson `writing-about-a-day` (R16);
    - В21 — epic E6 of `spec`: an optional part of a lesson's explanation, "a list of steps", by a minor change — the texts in order, and the app draws the numbers, and the search for numbers does not see them. Until it, the steps of a method are paragraphs without numbers (R14);
    - В22 — the 2026-11-07 addition to О-23: one text for all ages works when the sentences are simple, and where the topic is different, `age_categories` is enough; the module does not yet ask for texts of its own by style;
    - В23 — the 2026-11-07 amendment to R103 spec and repositories 4 spec, step 3: in a format transition nothing of what the simulator reads from the package is changed, except how it is written down, and the review of `modules` holds this; that is changed by a separate pull request after step 4, with the simulator. The 2026-11-08 amendment to R03;
  - **lessons in the engine** (R107 spec; R05 engine; the card architecture 3.8 spec): the proposal of `modules` on the band of a lesson is accepted — the band of a lesson is the set of the bands of its tasks, a lesson without tasks suits any band, and a lesson of another band does not hold the order. Lessons whose skills are not all `verdict` go by the graph, and where the graph gives no order, by the lesson's ID. This is how the lessons of `english-essays` go — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.9;
  - **the report of the eval runner** — the second 2026-11-08 amendment to R95 spec: it is checked by the recipe of `spec` `report-check`, which epic E7 of `spec` writes, and CI of `modules` calls it on the reports that it reads, from the same release (Д115 `spec`). This is epic E4 of table 2 of RUN.md;

  - R107 spec otherwise, the edits of the contract `cause.proto` and of concepts are about other components. They do not change the documents of `modules`.

## The rest of the port

**R20. The converter writes an answer that is not a number by its kind. A time on a clock `H:MM` and one word that stands in the question are a choice by hand: an answer slot `anywhere` with the check `choice`, which has its own five `options`. Everything else is a tapped choice. A percentage is `number` with `units: ["%"]`. An explanation that names the key of a choice the converter finds itself and refuses. The run of all the tasks that no batch names is the mode `--survey` and the recipe `just port-survey`. The pull requests of E3 gather in the branch `e3`, which stands in the ruleset of `main`.** The author's answers 2026-11-08 in the planning of T18 (RUN.md, T18, «Решено автором»), all four by the recommendations, and the rest by the plan that the author approved.
- **The kind of the answer** — the order of `answerOf`, by the correct option ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15):
  - the question "Which of these…" — a tapped choice, as in R12;
  - a number or a fraction in the format's notation — `number` or `fraction`, as in R12. A number with words after it — "42 cm", "4th", "44%" — is a number only when the batch gives the task `units` and the words are one of them. Words not from `units` are a refusal, as in R12, and without `units` such an option is not a number: "19 May", "3 and 6", "1 hour 20 minutes". The report names each answer by tapping that begins with a digit — "19 May", "014": so a batch that forgot `units` is seen too;
  - a time `^([01]?[0-9]|2[0-3]):[0-5][0-9]$` — before a number is read: otherwise "7:30" would be read as the number 7 with the words ":30" — and one word `^\p{L}+$`, other than "yes" and "no", that stands in the question as a whole word, with word boundaries ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20), — a choice by hand: `{binding: anywhere, check: {kind: choice, key, options, trap_values}}`, all five options in the check's `options`, the four wrong ones trap values;
  - everything else — a tapped choice, with five options: a word that is not in the question, "yes" and "no" — by hand the learner would write "yes, always", which is not among the options, — a phrase, a quantity of several parts, a date.

  Among the 726 tasks at 42b4408 (a count by jq 2026-11-08) these are 501 numbers and 7 fractions; 40 numbers with words are a number if the batch gives `units`, otherwise by tapping; by hand — 30 times and 45 words; by tapping — 75 other answers and 28 "Which of these…".
- **The price of a choice by hand.** The options are closed, so a value not among the five options is not in the choice's notation: `unreadable`, not `not_yet`. The learner is asked to write it again, and it gives no hint rung. A full stop at the end the normalization of a choice does not drop. So the overlay file has the field `answer_form` — a paragraph of `statement` after the question and the picture, saying in what form to write the answer: "A statement SHOULD say what form the answer takes" ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15). It is written by the content tasks where it is needed. The request to the format — a choice by hand where any other value gives `not_yet` — goes into the outcome of the epic, T43.
- **An explanation that names the key of a choice.** `task.no-leaks` does not look for the key of a tapped choice, nor for a key that stands in the statement, of any kind ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20). A word that is written by hand always stands in the question, by the rule above, and a time not always, and then `task.no-leaks` looks for its key. So for a tapped choice and for a word by hand an explanation of a wrong option that names the correct one the format's check will not find, and the converter looks for it — "the search for the words of the correct option" of R05, — at every choice, by one rule. The normal form is by the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20: step 1 of the normalization of numbers, NFKC `golang.org/x/text/unicode/norm`, dashes and slashes, spaces and simple lowercase. The key is looked for as a phrase with word boundaries. One found in an explanation and not rewritten in `trap_texts` is a refusal. A key of a choice in `givens`, `unknown` and `simpler_case` of the overlay file is a refusal too. And `answer_form` names the key of no answer: the paragraph stands in the statement, and a key that is in it the format's check would no longer look for anywhere else. There, too, the five options of a choice must differ in the normal form of a choice, `task.choice-options`.
- **Reading 42b4408.** The task's field `idea` is read and not carried over. The topic catalog `content/catalogs/topics.json` is read strictly: a field that the converter does not know is a refusal, and it knows all eight fields of 42b4408; the task's topic must be in it, and a task of a topic with `solved_from_picture: true` must have a picture. Nothing from the catalog goes into the module.
- **The run** — `port --standalone <clone> --survey --commit <sha> --except <batches folder> --module <folder> [--prove]`:
  - it takes each task of the clone that no batch of the folder names, without overlay files. The canvas is `handwriting_sheet` with `squares`, the skills have no techniques, there are no `units`, the picture has one stub `alt`, and an explanation that names the key of a choice the run writes and counts;
  - the outcome of each task — written, with the kind of answer, waiting for a picture of a kind that is not drawn yet, or a refusal. The summary is by kinds of answer and kinds of pictures. With `--prove` the test bench runs only the written tasks;
  - the codes — 0, 1 if the run refused a task, and 2 if it could not;
  - the recipe `just port-survey <folder> [commit]`, by default 42b4408, clones standalone as `port` does and calls `_port-survey` with the test bench. That one builds the converter as a binary — `go run` would turn code 2 into code 1 — and a new folder from the format examples `olympiad-math` and `LICENSE`, writes the tasks into it and calls `module-kit check`. The violations of `task.no-leaks` it counts — the overlay files of the content tasks fix them, — and any other violation and any warning is code 1;
  - the run of 2026-11-08: 586 tasks are written — 398 numbers, 7 fractions, 54 choices by hand and 127 by tapping; 140 wait for pictures: `lattice` 38, `clock` 36, `containers` 13, `grid` 11, `bars` 8, `piles` 7, `table` 7, `venn` 6, `number_line` 3, `balance`, `ring`, `shapes`, `stairs` and `tiles` 2 each, `calendar` 1. There are no refusals, and the test bench of 42b4408 proves all 586. Explanations that name the key of a choice — 94 in 57 tasks, and `module-kit check` finds 128 violations of `task.no-leaks` in 101 tasks, and no other violations or warnings.
- **The epic's branch** — `e3` from `main`, and `refs/heads/e3` stands in `conditions.ref_name.include` of the ruleset of `main`: the same required checks, without a second copy of the list. The pull requests of T18–T41 go into it, and T42 merges it into `main`. The rule `deletion` of the ruleset holds it too, so before deleting the branch T42 removes it from include. If something comes to `main` during the epic — a move of `spec`, Д5, — it is merged into `e3` by a merge commit. `release.yml` publishes after a green CI on `main` — `workflow_run` with `branches: [main]` — and by the button `workflow_dispatch` on the branch that is chosen. The dry run of a release on a pull request into `e3` publishes nothing, and the button on `e3` is not pressed: it would put out a release from the epic's branch.
- **The commit, push and pull request** of T18 are made by the executor — on the author's instruction, given after the task was checked, and only in it, as in T11. The executor also puts `e3` into the ruleset: the confirmation by rule 4 of RUN.md is given by the same instruction. The pull request of T18 goes into `e3`: T18 changes the `justfile`, which is in the release's `paths`, and in `main` this would give a release without changes of the modules (R17).

— *Why:*
- **By kind**: R76 spec requires handwriting, and with a tapped choice the learner guesses with a probability of 1/5 (R05). A time and a word from the question are written without options, while a word that is neither in the statement nor in the question, and a phrase, cannot be written without options.
- **A percentage is a number**: the check by value and the trap values that are numbers remain, and step 6 drops the sign, like any unit.
- **A number with words — by the batch's `units`**: the date "19 May" and "3 and 6" read as the notation of a number with a unit, and the words after a number are a unit only if the person who writes the batch says so.
- **The search for the key in the converter**: without it the explanation "so it is a Monday" after the wrong "Sunday" would reach the learner, and the format's check does not look for it.
- **The run by the converter's code, not by jq**: the count is the one that the batches will give, and T19–T21 run the run again for their pictures.
- **The branch in the ruleset of `main`**: one place holds the same list of checks.

— *Rejected:*
- everything by tapping — the argument above;
- everything by hand — the argument above;
- a percentage as `choice`;
- guessing the batch's `units` for a number with words from the option itself: "19 May" would become the number 19 with the unit May;
- the search for the key of a choice only by the hands of proofreading;
- the branch `e3` with a ruleset of its own: two copies of the list of checks would diverge; a branch without a ruleset: nobody would stop a merge with a red check;
- the tasks into `main` with a version of the module in each — this was rejected by the author's decision 4 in the breakdown of E3 (RUN.md, table 1).

— *Consequence:*
- `tools/port/`: `standalone.go` — `idea`, the topic catalog; `convert.go` — the kind of answer, the choice by hand, the search for the key, `answer_form`; `normal.go` — the normal forms; `survey.go` — the run; `row.go` — `notDrawn`; `batch.go` — `answer_form`; `main.go` — the flags of the run; the Go tests and `test/port.test.mjs`. `go.mod` — `golang.org/x/text` as a direct dependency;
- `justfile` — `port-survey` and `_port-survey`; CLAUDE.md, Environment;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2, 3.3 and 3.5;
- the 2026-11-08 amendments to R05 and R12;
- RUN.md, T18 — «Открыто»: the run's module in T21 needs two new skills, and the request to the format about a choice by hand is T43.

— RUN.md, T18; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2, 3.3 and 3.5.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T33):* **a batch may make the choice of a task tapped, though its key is a word of the question: `tapped: true` on the task.** In `game-56-d5-1` the key is "Ben", a name from the question, and the wrong options are arguments: "Ann, since she goes first", "Ann, by sending home the children on the side of 3 first". A child who thinks that Ann will win will write "Ann", and there is no such option: a choice by hand keeps the options closed, and the answer would become `unreadable` without a hint rung. So a choice is by hand only when the wrong options are answers that the learner writes themselves, like "It is impossible to tell" or "red or blue"; where they are arguments, the batch sets `tapped`. Of the 75 tasks of the module that were written by hand before, only this one has arguments instead of answers. Rejected: a rule in the converter by which a choice is by hand only when each option is one word or a time — it would take the hand away from 43 tasks where the learner writes "It is impossible to tell", "Ben and Kim" or "5:60" without trouble; guessing arguments by a comma or by "since" — a rule from signs, not from meaning. — RUN.md, T33; `tools/port/batch.go`, `convert.go`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09 (T49):* **a word by hand has `notation: word`, right after `kind`, as in the format example `who-is-shorter.yaml`.** Format 0.4 answered the request В28: with the mark any one word is read, a word not from the options is "not yet" with a hint rung, not `unreadable`, and a full stop, `!`, `?` and spaces at the end the normalization drops ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15; R108 spec, R111 spec). The key is one word of letters, and the other options and the trap values may be phrases, like "It is impossible to tell", — in 29 of the 44 tasks. The mark is given to all 44 words by hand — 34 in `ord-*`, 7 in `par-*` and 3 days of the week in `cal-*`, — and nothing else in their files changes: the key, the options, the trap values and the texts are the same, and the rule of which word is written by hand is the same as above. A time stays a choice with closed options until T50, and a tapped choice carries no mark: it has no `notation`. The converter compares the options of a word the way the mark reads them — with the end cut off — and refuses if two coincide or one becomes empty, like "?": otherwise the task would be rejected only by `task.choice-options` in `module-kit check`. `module-kit diff` names each of the 44 edits major — the mark changes what decides the verdict ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27), — and `olympiad-math` moves to 0.3.0. Rejected: the mark on a time too — the format lets it through only with a key that is a word (`task.choice-key`), and a time will get its own kind; leaving the words without the mark until the end of the epic — a learner who erred with a word not from the options would hear "write it again" instead of a hint. — RUN.md, T49; `tools/port/convert.go`, `normal.go`, `choice_test.go`; `test/port.test.mjs`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10 (T50):* **a time on a clock is the check `time`, without `options`, and a key after 12:59 also has the trap value `twelve-hours-off`: the same time on a 12-hour dial.** Format 0.4 answered the request В29 with the kind `time`: any `H:MM` is read, a value is correct whose hours and minutes as numbers equal the key's, and the kind does not count the sum of minutes — `5:60` is not `6:00` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15; R108 spec, R111 spec). There is no parameter of 12 or 24 hours: "where a learner answers by the 12-hour dial, `modules` sets a trap, `9:10` for `21:10`" (R111 spec).
- **The key** — the hours without a leading zero, as the key's schema requires: `07:30` the converter writes as `7:30`. The keys of the 30 tasks `clk-*` were already so. In the explanations and the texts of the overlay files the converter looks for the key with a leading zero too, `07:30`, as the format's leak search finds it ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20).
- **The wrong options** are trap values, as they were, but the converter compares them the way the kind reads them: two options with one time, like `7:00` and `07:00`, are a refusal. An option not in the notation of a read time — the hours have one or two digits, the minutes two or three without a zero in front — cannot be a trap value: its trap goes into the task's `traps`, and the text does not, as with a number. In the 30 tasks all the options are in the notation, and the `trap_values` of each are four values, as they were, "5:60" and "10:100" among them.
- **The dial's value** is written by the converter by a rule: for a key with hours from 13 to 23 — the time with the hours 12 less, and for a key of the first hour after midnight, `0:MM`, — `12:MM`, with the trap `twelve-hours-off` and one text for all the tasks, without numbers: "That is the time on a clock face, which starts its hours again at noon. Count the hours of the afternoon on from noon." It is not there if such a time already stands among the options. In the module these are five tasks, and in each the statement counts the time by 24 hours: `clk-34-d3-3` — 7:40 for 19:40, `clk-34-d3-4` — 9:10 for 21:10, `clk-34-d4-3` — 4:05 for 16:05, `clk-56-d4-2` — 11:00 for 23:00, `clk-56-d5-3` — 1:30 for 13:30. The trap is the module's own entry of the catalog (the T50 amendment to R23).
- **`answer_form`** — "Write the time as hours and minutes, like 9:15." — stays: the format still asks the statement to name the form of the answer, and the 12-hour notation is caught by the trap value. The search for the key in the explanations, `givens`, `unknown` and `simpler_case` is the same as for a choice.
- **The run** names the kind `time` separately in the summary: "number, fraction, time, choice by hand, choice tapped".

`module-kit diff` names each of the 30 edits major — the check kind changes what decides the verdict, — and `olympiad-math` stays at 0.3.0, which T49 raised. Rejected: the dial's value by hand in the overlay files — five copies of one text; for keys from 1:00 to 12:59 — on the dial they are the same; changing the `answer_form` of five tasks to "like 19:15" — the form of the answer is not a trap, and a learner who still writes 7:40 will get advice. — RUN.md, T50; `tools/port/convert.go`, `normal.go`, `survey.go`, `choice_test.go`, `survey_test.go`; `test/port.test.mjs`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5.

**R21. The `alt` of a task that is solved from the picture names each fact that its solver reads — walls, lines, cells, numbers on tiles, stairs and jumps, — without the key and without a solving step. The converter checks that such an `alt` names each text of the picture, and that no `alt` names the key unless the question names it. The geometry is held by proofreading. The converter draws four kinds of tasks solved from the picture: `lattice`, `tiles`, `shapes` and `stairs`.** The decisions in the planning of T21 were taken by the executor 2026-11-08 on the author's instruction: «там где требуется планирование — планируй и сам выбирай наиболее разумные варианты» ("where planning is needed, plan and choose the most reasonable options yourself"). Both by the recommendations of RUN.md, T21.
- **A task that is solved from the picture** is one whose solver `content/examples/solvers/<id>.star` of standalone declares `solve` with a second parameter, `solve(options, picture)`. There are 48 such at 42b4408, and the run counts them. The converter reads the solver of each task that it writes; a task without a solver file does not read the picture, and a file that is missing the test bench will find.
- **The rule of `alt`** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5). The data of such a task stand only on the picture, and the `alt` is read by a screen reader, and only it remains where the picture is not seen ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14). So the `alt` says in words everything that the solver reads: the size of the field, each line and cell, each number and label, each stair and jump. It names neither the key nor a solving step.
- **The converter's check:**
  - for a task that is solved from the picture, the `alt` of the statement's picture names each text that the picture writes: a label, a number on a tile, the total of a row of figures, the label of a stair. The text is looked for as a phrase in the normal form of the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20, like the key of a choice (R20). A label of capital Latin letters is looked for case-sensitively: the article "a" does not stand in for the label "A", though an article at the start of a phrase the check cannot tell from the label. The numbers that the picture numbers itself — the digits of a dial, the ticks of a line, the days, the places of a ring, — the description does not give, and the check does not require them. A clock does not write the time — the hands show it, and the `alt` says it as a fact. A missing text is a refusal;
  - for any task, the `alt` of the statement's picture does not name the key unless the question names it or the picture itself writes it. A key that stands in the `alt` the leak check counts as shown with the task and looks for nowhere else ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20). A named key is a refusal. A picture that writes the key itself already shows it, and the `alt` names it like any text of the picture. Then the report says that the key is looked for nowhere, and what gives it away is carried by the `leak_phrases` of the overlay file. There are 11 such tasks at 42b4408;
  - lines, cells, stairs and jumps are geometry, and what the `alt` says about them only proofreading holds.
- **Four kinds** — with the same structure as T19 and T20, checked against standalone's service:
  - `lattice` — squared paper up to 8 × 8 cells: shaded cells, lines from corner to corner, dashed lines, points at corners with labels, and labels in cells. Everything that the service checks is checked: the lines join two different corners, none is drawn twice, solid ones meet only at the corners of the grid and do not run along each other, and the label of a point gets the cell, of the four at its corner, that the label of a cell does not hold. The places of the labels — by the same method as the service's;
  - `tiles` — identical regular polygons cut into triangles, side by side or in a ring. A row goes straight, up to 6 tiles and up to 4 hexagons, and a ring closes — by the same count of turns as the service's. The tiles are laid out by geometry: the next one lies on the side `joint` of the previous one and faces it with its first side;
  - `shapes` — rows of figures — a triangle, a square, a circle, a rhombus — with a total on the right;
  - `stairs` — a staircase to the right from the floor without numbers of the stairs, labels above the stairs, and jumps as arcs above the labels with an arrow to where the jump lands.
- **The run's module** `_port-survey` gets two new skills, `counting-figures` and `geometry-cubes-paper`, if their topics are in the clone, while the graph of the format example does not have them. This is «Открыто» of T18. The skills go at the end of the graph, under `skills:`, and the recipe checks that this is its last key.
- **The run of 2026-11-08.** `just port-survey` at 42b4408 writes all 726 tasks, and not one waits for a picture. `flags` the converter does not draw: not one task has this kind.

— *Why:*
- **Facts in the `alt`**: without them a task solved from the picture cannot be solved by a learner with a screen reader.
- **The check of the picture's texts**: it can be made exact — a text is either named or not, — and it catches an omission that proofreading 48 tasks easily misses.
- **The check of the key in the `alt`**: otherwise a key that the `alt` named by mistake would stop being looked for in `givens`, explanations and hints, and no check would find this leak.
- **The solver, not the topic**: of the 48 tasks solved from the picture, 18 stand in earlier topics, and only the solver says that a task reads the picture.

— *Rejected:*
- a short `alt` without facts — the argument above;
- facts in the `alt`, and what cannot be done without — as text of the statement: standalone's statement at 42b4408 is written for the picture, and an edit of it is no longer a port;
- a check of the geometry in the `alt`: a wall, a line and a cell are named in words in different ways, and matching the words against coordinates would give false refusals;
- the flag "from the picture" by the topic or by the field `idea`: the topic does not cover 18 tasks of earlier topics, and `idea` is a field of ideas, not of the solver.

— *Consequence:*
- `tools/port/`: `lattice.go`, `tiles.go`, `shapes.go`, `stairs.go`, `labels.go`; `convert.go` — `altOf`; `standalone.go` — the task's solver; `picture.go` — `polygon`, `dashed`; the Go tests; `justfile` — the two skills of the run's module;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5 — the rule of `alt`;
- the content tasks T22–T34 write the `alt` of the tasks solved from the picture by this rule.

— RUN.md, T21; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5.

**R22. A template of standalone becomes a template of the format in `tasks/<name>.yaml`, by hand: the ID is the name of standalone's template, the parameters are the numbers of the reference task's statement, the solver is standalone's program under the format's dialect. The numbers of the reference task are one of the instances, and a test checks that this instance gives its key. A template whose solver reads the picture is not carried over.** Decided in the planning of T35 2026-11-08 — by the executor, on the author's instruction (RUN.md, T35).

- **The ID and the link with the reference task.** The ID of a template is its name in standalone, `content/solvers/<topic>/<name>.star`: `neighbours`, `boxes`, like `number-thought-of` in the format example. The names of the 39 templates at 42b4408 do not repeat. `band`, `difficulty`, the skill, the techniques — of the reference task from which the template is generalized (the line "# From reference task <id>." of its file). The header of the file names the file of standalone at 42b4408 and the reference task, carries the solver's MIT notice, like the format example, and the line "# The numbers of reference task <id>: <name> <number>, …" — the numbers of the reference task.
- **Parameters, domains, constraint.** The parameters are the uppercase constants of standalone's template that the statement shows. Constants that the statement does not show stay numbers of the text, like the first floor in `neighbours`. The domain of each parameter is around the number of the reference task, so that its numbers are one of the instances, and the instances are alike in difficulty ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 23): the constraint cuts off the too easy and the too hard. The trap values are derived numbers, the errors of the reference task, and the constraint keeps them whole and apart from the key and from each other.
- **The solver** is standalone's program, and `solve` gets a dictionary of the statement's numbers and returns a dictionary of results, not the letter of an option ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 24). The helpers of standalone's sandbox — `permutations`, `product`, `gcd` — and `sum` with `**` are not in the format's dialect: code of its own replaces them, an odometer or a loop. The key is a result of the solver. The texts that the learner sees before solving — traps, `simpler_case`, `givens` — take only parameters and derived numbers, and a result stands only in the key, in the solution and in the leak phrases.
- **The check.** `module-kit check` builds each instance, runs the solver and checks the leaks on each. And `test/templates.test.mjs` builds the package and for each template finds the instance with the numbers of the reference task: its key, band and difficulty must match the key, band and difficulty of the reference task. So the template's solver still proves the answer of the task from which the template grew, as standalone itself checks it.
- **What is not carried over.** The six templates whose solver reads the picture — `sticks-square`, `shapes-rows`, `squares-on-lines`, `triangles-on-lines`, `fold-net` and `fold-sheet`: for a task solved from the picture its numbers are the picture itself, and the picture path of a template is one for all the instances (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R05). Their tasks stay fixed. The request to the format about the picture of an instance goes into the outcome of the epic, T43. Nor is a template carried over that has no number to change within the band: `arrangements` — its parameters are the names of children, and the only number, 3 chairs, at 4 gives a key equal to a trap value, and at 5 takes the task far beyond band 1–2. The reason for each such template is written in «Сделано» of its task.

— *Why:*
- **The name as the ID**: the format example does so, and the ID shows which file of standalone became the template.
- **By hand, not by the converter**: the converter takes a task of standalone — the question, the options, the explanations, — and a template of standalone is a program that has neither a statement nor options. The converter cannot translate Starlark and invent domains for the author, and describing a template with a separate overlay file would mean repeating in its own words the format's field `template`.
- **The numbers of the reference task among the instances**: then there is an answer known in advance, and the test checks the solver against it. Without this the key of each instance would be checked only by the solver itself.

— *Rejected:*
- an ID of the form `gaps-tpl-neighbours`: longer, and the format example does not do so;
- the converter writes the templates from an overlay file — the argument above;
- carrying over the templates solved from the picture with one picture for all the instances: the picture would argue with the numbers of the instance; carrying over only their numbers that the picture does not hold: a task solved from the picture has no such numbers;
- `arrangements` with the number of children from 3 to 5: at 4 the key, 12, coincides with the number of seatings where Ann and Ben are side by side — a trap value, — and at 5 the key, 72, is not a task of grades 1–2.

— *Consequence:*
- `modules/olympiad-math/tasks/`: `neighbours`, `positions`, `digits`, `boxes`, `draws` (T35); T36 and T37 carry over the rest by this decision;
- `test/templates.test.mjs` — the check of the key of the reference task's instance;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5 — the row "template" of the table "what becomes what";
- RUN.md, T43 — the request to the format about the picture of a template's instance.

— RUN.md, T35; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T36), by the executor, on the author's instruction:* **nor is a template carried over whose answer is a choice: a time, a day of the week, a name, one of the options.** A result of the solver stands only in the key and in the trap values of the check of a number or a fraction, in the solution and in the leak phrases ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 24), and the key of a choice and its five options would have to be built from derived numbers — by a formula that standalone's template does not have: its value is in the search. In T36 these are `reachable` (an option), `orders` (a name), `minutes` (a time) and `weekdays` (a day of the week). Nor is a template carried over without numbers that the statement shows — `whole-day` and `speakers`, — nor `round-table`: the words "Both my neighbours are liars" settle the number of knights only at a table of 3, 4 and 5 places, and there the keys are 1, 2 and 2. Rejected: building the key and the options of a time from derived numbers — the hours, the tens and the units of the minutes of each of the five options — that is five formulas per template, and standalone's solver does not check them; `round-table` at a table of 4 and 5 places — two instances with one key, 2, give the task nothing that the reference task does not have; `round-table` with 3 places — the key 1 coincides with a trap value of the reference task. — RUN.md, T36.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08 (T37), by the executor, on the author's instruction:* **nor a template whose numbers do not change within the task: the names of cells, a list of divisors, the fractions of the statement — or whose key in each instance equals a number of the statement.** In T37 these are `figure` — its numbers are the names of the figure's cells, "A1", "B3"; `search` — its numbers are the list of divisors 2–6, and another list gives the same key or another task; `share-of-all` — the numbers of its statement are fractions, 2/5, a half, 1/6, and most combinations of their parts give no whole number of books, and the solver finds no whole one; `pieces` — a tile 2 by n divides in half in exactly n ways, so the key of each instance equals the number of columns of the statement, and a learner who copied it would solve the task; `one-pile` — the winning move for the bags of 8, 15 and 22 is always one, 1, and the statement names it among the moves "1, 3 or 4", for other bags the move is different, and the trap values — the other moves — would have to be known in advance, without the solver. `two-piles` is not carried over by the T36 amendment: its answer is a move, a choice. A number that standalone's template writes inside `fits`, not as an uppercase constant, is a parameter too if the statement shows it: the total of the baskets in `split`, the eaten apples in `counts`. Rejected: `pieces` with rows and columns together — for 3 by 4 the key is 19, a task of the wrong difficulty; `share-of-all` with one changing fraction — the key stays a fraction, and its irreducible form would have to be held by derived numbers. — RUN.md, T37.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10 (T51):* **a template whose reference task carries a hint of standalone keeps it as the leading question `leading_question` — with placeholders in place of the reference's numbers, like `givens`.** There are eight such templates: in `changes`, `counts`, `for-every`, `split`, `three-groups` and `two-groups` the hint has no numbers of the reference, and it stands word for word, while in `groups` "97" is `{seat}`, and in `what-is-left` "30" is `{left}`. A line of the template's header under the line of the reference's numbers names whose hint it is. The test of the templates now checks the question too: the instance with the reference's numbers gives the question of the reference task, and where a template has no question, the reference has none either. Rejected: templates without a question — an instance would be poorer than its reference task. The author's decision 2026-11-10 in the planning of T51, by the recommendation. — RUN.md, T51; `modules/olympiad-math/tasks/` — eight templates; `test/templates.test.mjs`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10 (T52):* **the picture of the reference task is the picture of each instance of the template: a path with placeholders of its parameters and a file for each instance, all the instances.** Format 0.4 let the placeholders of parameters and derived numbers into the picture path of a template, and the module puts in the file of each instance, and `task.known-pictures` holds it ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 23; R108 spec) — the answer to the request В30. So the pictures that T37 had left out came back: of the statement in `for-every`, `split`, `groups` and `two-groups`, of the solution in `changes` and `groups`. The paths — all the parameters of the template in the order of its `parameters`: `pictures/split-{total}.png`, `pictures/for-every-{daisies}.png`, `pictures/groups-{seat}-{size}.png`, `pictures/groups-solution-{seat}-{size}.png`, `pictures/two-groups-{total}-{dog}-{cat}.png`, `pictures/changes-solution-{pct}-{end}.png`; `alt` — the reference's `alt` from the overlay file, with placeholders. The key of no instance gets into the path: `task.no-leaks` holds this on each instance. The solution of `changes` and `groups` also got the last line of the reference — "{end} ÷ {parts_left} × {parts_all} = {price}" and "{last_full} ÷ {size} + 1 = {row}". The numbers that the picture needs and the template did not compute are derived numbers: in `groups` the places of the rows on the pictures and the number of the next row, in `changes` the old price and the number of shares; a result of the solver the picture does not hold, and the port does not know it.
- **All the instances** — the author's decision 2026-11-10 in the planning of T52: `groups` — 374 instances, two pictures each, `two-groups` — 94, `changes` — 7, `split` and `for-every` — 4 each, in total 857 files, ≈ 10 MB: the folder `media/pictures/` grew from 2.8 to 13 MB. Rejected: narrowing `groups` and `two-groups` to ≈ 24 instances — ≈ 1.3 MB; the picture of the statement of `groups` only by the size of a row and without a picture of the solution.
- **The size** of a picture is one for all the instances, like its `width` and `height` in the template: the largest of its drawings, and each drawing stands in the middle of a white field. The port refuses if the template gives another.

Rejected: one picture per template without numbers — the picture of the reference shows the numbers of the statement, and without them it is not the same; pictures by hand — 857 files. — RUN.md, T52; `modules/olympiad-math/tasks/` — five templates; `tools/port/template-pictures/olympiad-math/`; `test/templates.test.mjs`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10 (T53), by the executor, on the author's instruction:* **a template whose solver reads the picture is carried over if the picture can be built from the numbers that it shows: these numbers are its parameters, the function `figure` of its solver builds the picture from them in standalone's schema, `solve` computes on it with standalone's code, and the port draws the same picture for each instance** (the T53 amendment to R12). The ban of R22 rested on one picture path for all the instances, and T52 gave a picture to each instance. So the learner sees exactly the figure on which the solver found the key. The picture of the solution is the function `solution_figure`. `solve` gets the numbers that stand in the `alt` of the statement's picture: `module-kit` passes it those too.
- **Three are carried over** — 52 instances, 104 pictures, ≈ 1.1 MB:
  - `shapes-rows` from `tri-34-d3-6` — the sums of the three rows from 7 to 11, from 5 to 9 and from 6 to 10, with an even sum and three different whole figures above 0: 39 instances, keys 9–15;
  - `squares-on-lines` from `fig-56-d1-1` — the width of the rectangle from 3 to 7 cells at a height of 3, so that the solution always has three sizes of square: 5 instances;
  - `sticks-square` from `enum-34-d3-6` — the long stick from 3 to 5, the short one shorter, from 1: 8 instances. standalone's lattice is no wider than 8 cells, so sticks of length 3 lie as on the reference's picture, and longer ones one per row, with the short ones in two columns on the right. The long stick from 3 leaves the simple case of the task, four of 2 and four of 1, outside the instances.

  The traps are the errors of the reference, as derived numbers. The paths — by the rule of T52: `pictures/shapes-rows-{first}-{second}-{third}.png`, `pictures/squares-on-lines-{width}.png`, `pictures/sticks-square-{long}-{short}.png` and the same with `-solution`. The key of each instance is greater than any number of its path.
- **Three are not carried over**, and their tasks stay fixed:
  - `fold-net` — its uppercase `ASKED`, the face that is asked about, from 1 to 6, gives three tasks, each in both directions: on the staircase net 1 and 4, 2 and 5, 3 and 6 are opposite. In the reference's traps each face has a reason of its own — "2 touches 1 along an edge", "3 is not opposite 1", — and the neighbours on the net differ for different faces, so the template would have to give all four one phrase and one trap of the catalog. An instance would be poorer than the reference task — the same argument as in the T51 amendment;
  - `fold-sheet` — the statement has no numbers, and the hole can be punched only in the cells of the last stack: (3,2) and (3,3) have 8 layers each, and another cell would give the same key;
  - `triangles-on-lines` — the statement has no numbers, and the figure of the reference — the midlines and the median of a right triangle — belongs to no family with a number in which it would be an instance.

Rejected: `sticks-square` with the reference's layout at any lengths — with a long stick of 4 and 5 the lattice is wider than 8 cells; `fold-net` with traps without reasons; a new family of figures for `triangles-on-lines` — the reference would not be its instance, and the test of the reference would have nothing to check. — RUN.md, T53; `modules/olympiad-math/tasks/` — three templates; `tools/port/template-pictures/olympiad-math/`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5.
